### 17.3 Corrections to this plan (what the builds and verifiers proved)

1. 6.5: pausing stops the worker (so it does not let it "get up to 30 minutes ahead"); "Use the fast model from here" recomputes from the last checkpoint, not from this moment; the footnote says last 10 seconds. (Edited inline above.)
2. 5.3 and 11: the statistics chunk is 15.0 kB gzip (plus `runEvents` 1.1), not 6 to 8; the model card is its own lazy chunk (18.4 kB); the entry fell (the build now splits `content`, `recordKit`, `store` and `types` out of it). `EngineInfo.perf.embers` is always null; `perf.history` holds only filled samples; the events drain by a size budget (about 750 characters per snapshot).
3. 6.2: the Auto cut-off is about 198.4 ms per 3-D air step for every run of 36 minutes or longer (Setup offers 1 to 12 h); below 36 minutes it is 428571 / duration in seconds. On this build machine the steady median is 20.9 ms and the warm-up mean 50.7 ms. A Performance setting changed mid-run maps Balanced to the standard tier outright.
4. 6.3 and check-list item 3: the signal needs 22 to 27 s after Play (about 33 s when pressed during spin-up), not about 20 s: the plan's own constants (15 s grace plus a 6 s or 10 s hold) cannot give 20. On the real engine there was no false positive at any speed the engine keeps up with and no flapping.
5. 6.4: the live-region announcement is a separate visually hidden `role=status` span; the button has `aria-haspopup` and `aria-expanded`; the ring and run are drawn in `--watch-ink`.
6. 9b: the Layers panel is 940 DOM nodes with the row open (the row is 25 nodes, not 12); the dusk step when cast shadows switch off is +0.9 (Daylight) and +1.1 (Brighter) luma; the Daylight map at -1.5 degrees is brighter than at +10 (a 30 degree sun, by design).
7. 9c: `guessQuality` lives in `src/render/qualityGuess.ts`; the heads-up also shows for Auto under Detail on a Low phone; `navigator.deviceMemory` is a coarse bucket and never 6 GB, so the High guess needs the 8 GB class.
8. 9e: the icon row writes times as the panel does ("lit at 14:05 (+0:00)"); the home hit radius is 6 to 14 px following the drawn size, not a fixed 14.
9. 9d.3.4: R follows the MESH slope with bands 48-58 / 45-55 / 42-52 degrees per tier (the default 52-62 gave Tomah Low no rock cells) and canopy cover fades out on walls (53 to 90 % of cells over 75 degrees have cover of 0.5 or more in the coarse canopy data); the photo-or-rock choice still follows the mesh slope, with the mask adding the detail. The mesh slope is a per-vertex attribute (`aSlope`), not a texture, so the terrain shader uses 11 of 16 samplers. The insolation overlay reads the unsharpened terrain.
10. 9d.3.2 and 9d.6: the mesh despike clips real summits at High (largest moves 26.6 m Katoomba, 42.5 m Grose); a 150 m step keeps 75.1 degrees at the worst phase on the 22 m mesh and 62.5 on the 40 m mesh (means 80.1 and 73.2), not 80 and 72 at every phase; Tomah's High mesh passes by a hair (0.3045 % of edges over 70 degrees against 0.3, steepest edge 79.2 against 79). "Sparse shrubs on ledges" is not built beyond rejection and thinning of trees.
11. 11: cliff CPU in the app (production build, Katoomba): sharpen 46 ms, mask 78, mesh 25 to 37, lighting 28; **the mask misses its 150 ms phone budget by estimate** (about 200 to 400 ms at 2.7 times; the mesh is also over 100 ms, accepted). Memory: `cliff-main` is 7.9 MB at 9 km (the rock copy plus the arrays Three.js keeps behind the textures), about 13 MB while the scenario is set up (a verifier measured +13 MB at High and +24 MB at Low at the peak). The terrain shader costs +19 to 30 % over no cliff code on software rendering (phone cost unknown).
12. 9d.4 and check-list item 7: gully climbing, the "rock band" card and the bare-rock share are H-sim (0.3.1); for 0.3.0 only the drawing, the statistics Terrain row and the Terrain card sentence apply. The fire's wall rule is not only "over 45 degrees on a 30 m mean": it is Landform Cliff (over 45 degrees on the 20 or 30 m fire grid), or at least 30 % of 10 m sub-cells over 60 degrees, or a sub-cell P90 of 60 degrees or more, and fuel inference also calls over 55 degrees non-fuel. The "gorges-and-cliffs card" of research note 06 is the spot variant of "Fire crossed the break" (non-fuel or water runs of 10 m or more).
13. 6.6: the pace episode's leading reason is written once per slow spell, on the first event after it is known.
14. Live data: only `src/data/canopy.ts` uses the GeoTIFF reader, only for the Meta / WRI canopy-height COG (Deflate with predictor 2, verified in research note 08 section 3.8 for one tile, Katoomba 311230121). Other tiles and any re-encoding by Meta are unverified (no network here); an unsupported compression shows as "the canopy service did not answer".

### 17.4 Known limitations and what is left

- The tier line stays "Auto: ... being timed" after Build until the first snapshot after Play (the host posts the first snapshot before spin-up), so the model card and the statistics screen cannot show the measured "Detailed, N ms" before Play. Predates 0.3.0; the fix needs the host to refresh the engine info after spin-up.
- "Play at N" takes the highest preset at or below the measured capacity with no margin; a growing fire can push it back under. The landscape drawer needs a scroll to reach Settings, the new row and New scenario (it already did for Settings). A failed lazy chunk cannot be retried on the same web page (Chromium caches the failure); the APK serves chunks locally.
- The fflate shim does not check Adler-32, accepts raw deflate and returns empty output for empty input.
- Cliff drawing: faces under about 60 m read as ramps on every tier; at eye level the 22 m facets show; top-down faces are mottled; a shaded wall without the photo goes charcoal (never black); shimmer on faces at 3.5 km is modest (non-edge pixels over 6 levels 0.5 to 2.1 %, against 0.0 to 0.01 % before); Low-tier Tomah draws rock on 20 % of its sharpened walls (71 % at High). A fade of the 25 m shelf and the stains by `fwidth(vZ)` would help the shimmer.
- Nothing about phones is measured: GPU time, shader compile time, the 11-sampler terrain shader on real GPUs, memory at the peak, the statistics screen's phone rows (Client Hints, battery, WebGPU, refresh rate), the 22 px and 14 px hit radii, the pace thresholds. Phone check-list 1 (section 12) is the way to find out; copy the statistics afterwards (frame rate, memory, sharpen, mask and mesh times, the Terrain row).
- 0.3.1: H-sim (needs the S6 re-label by someone who knows the escarpments), the speed test button. Open owner questions: drop the in-thread engine (140.6 kB) and the demo engine (15.0 kB) from the APK; whether the mask's CPU budget (150 ms) or the mesh's (100 ms) is worth optimising before 0.3.1.

### 17.5 Decisions taken for you in 0.3.0 (change any of them)

The in-thread engine and the demo engine stay in the APK (D10 default). The night look's target sun is 30 degrees (S4). Mesh decimation of about 300 ms on a phone is accepted. H-sim stays out of 0.3.0 (D17). The home hit radius follows the drawn size. Commit trailers carry no model name.


## 18. 0.4.0 as built (2026-10-15)

**Status.** Workstreams C (the air data foundation) and D (Air Slice, side view) are built and merged, with the bundle budget unchanged
(the owner said to reuse it, so the panel is a lazy chunk). Every lane is checked by an independent verifier on the real app after the merge
(the verifier columns below are from their reports; two fix rounds followed them, the history round and the slice-UI round, and what each did
about a finding is written in the same row, in 18.3 and in 18.4; the verifiers are named for what they checked: v-airdata the air data, v-slice the slice's maths and panel, v-tool the tool, the curtain and the Layers panel, v-cross the whole after the merge); all of it runs on software rendering, **nothing has been seen on a phone**. Gate results are in 18.2. E (the horizontal wind slice and glyphs, 0.5.0), the time-lapse recorder (0.4.1) and H-sim (0.3.1) are not in 0.4.0.
Where 18.3 contradicts sections 7 and 8, 18.3 wins.

### 18.1 What was built, and what the verifiers found

| Lane | Built | Verifier verdict | Defects the verifiers found (fixed unless stated) |
|---|---|---|---|
| contract (D) | types, constants and doc comments only, no behaviour: `render/slice/types.ts` (the conventions and the signature of every pure function) and `ui/screens/sim/sliceTypes.ts` (the tool and the panel); the nested optional `LayerState.crossSection` fields `a`, `b`, `preset`, `field`; `SceneViewApi.slice?` and `onSlice?`; `SceneLegends.slice?`; `UiState.slice?`; a comment-only `slice.css` | no verifier of its own (types, constants and comments only); read by every other lane's verifier | none reported against it |
| airdata (C) | the terrain-following air view with `layout`, `kind`, `topM`, `baseM` and `time` from both engines (fresh copies of the native arrays, no resample, no zeros under a hill); the fast tier's diagnosed 3-D wind (stamp-blended u, v, w; no temperature, no smoke); sampler v2 (`windP`, `fieldsP`, both layouts, allocation-free); `airKind`, `airLayout`, `airHas`, `hasAirWind`, `cellHeightAsl`, `airTime`, `airAgeS`, `AIR_STALE_S`; `has3dAtmosphere` now means prognostic and `hasAirWind` means a wind to slice; `keyframeBytesModel` and the memory rows; the wind speed heat map on the fast tier; the statistics and model-card rows; the oracle, purity, perf and transfer tests; `e2e/air-view.spec.ts` | v-airdata: pass with fixes | `windP` and `fieldsP` allocated whenever V8 did not inline their helper (48 and 80 bytes a call with inlining off, and the allocation test failed about once in four runs; fixed: each blend is handed back through a scratch array, and `atmosphereSampler.alloc.test.ts` runs with inlining off: 0.14 bytes a call). The verifier wrote a second oracle for the edges the first did not reach (`airView.edges.test.ts`: column nodes, the edge and corners, level centres, below the first level and above the top, a cliff step, sub-stamps, a wind edit, a whole slice, empty and NaN fields; worst difference 1.9e-6 m/s against a tolerance of 1e-3) and it holds. Found, and stated in 18.4: the published 10 m wind is not the wind the fire feels on a calm night. A fast-tier keyframe was much heavier than in 0.3.0 (1,310,636 B against 824,636 B), so the history of a small phone thinned early (a 1 GB phone kept 33 keyframes of a 6 hour run, 40 min apart at the widest, where 0.3.0 kept 55, 10 min apart): fixed by the history round, 18.3 item 25. Nits: the boxed `wind()` and `surfaceWind()` allocate about 62 bytes a call when not inlined (as before 0.4.0; 18.3 item 1); more smoke puffs sit below the 10 m ground than in 0.3.0 (18.4); fast-tier wording left stale (the Wind streaks text, three comments, the wind speed map's own words) is corrected in the second docs round |
| slicecore (D, pure) | `src/render/slice/`: `sampleSlice`, `sectionFromEndpoints`, `resolveSlice`, `slicePresetStatus`, `niceTicks`, `flow2d`, `probe`, the lattice reader, `createSliceUpdater` and test fixtures; the oracle against both engines (to 3e-6 m/s), the perf budget and heap-flat tests | v-slice: pass. The maths is exact: a slice equals the engines' own `sample()` to 1.6e-6 m/s | none in the maths. Findings about the presets, fixed in the slice-UI round (18.3 item 26): Along and Across took the forecast's 10 m direction at the shown time and not the model's wind (after a wind edit of 14 m/s from the east at the fire, the wind 30 m up was 12.7 m/s from about 95 degrees and the preset still ran towards 130 degrees), so they now follow the model's wind at the middle of the line, 30 m up; *Me to fire* was enabled with a position and no fire and then said so on the canvas, so it is disabled, with its reason, until there is a fire |
| curtain (D, scene) | one `SliceUpdater` owned by the scene, `SceneViewApi.slice()` and `onSlice()`; the curtain draws `SliceData` (`crossSectionColour.ts`); `ALONG_RAMP`, the log `SMOKE_RAMP`, `sliceFieldScale`, `sliceLegend`; `render/sliceFields.ts` (which chips an air can give, and every "diagnosed" and "needs the 3-D atmosphere" sentence); the catalog's `crossSection` and `windSpeed` entries on any 3-D wind; the Layers panel's six chips and the diagnosed-wind note in place of the old need-3-D callout; the map legend following the chip; "Show me"; the 2-D mock drawing the line; colour-vision tests for the new ramps and, for the first time, the two old ones | v-tool: pass with findings (the curtain and the Layers panel) | none broke the curtain; v-tool added `e2e/air-curtain-gpu.spec.ts` (60 changes of section, line, length, preset and chip leave the renderer's geometry and texture counts where the warm-up left them). Findings (v-tool), fixed in the slice-UI round: the Layers compass read "Towards N 0" with a line placed by the tool and, turned, moved the line to a stored centre instead of turning it (it now shows the line's own bearing and swings the line about its middle, 18.3 item 27); with the panel open an update cost about 14 ms more at a 4x throttle, because the panel repainted inside it (the repaint now takes the next display frame, 18.3 item 18); the Layers panel said "section" and announced a "cross-section" where the tool said "slice" (it says "slice" now, but for one note, 18.4) |
| slicetool (D, tool) | the Air slices tool: the Tools entry, `sliceToolModel` (the placement state machine and every word of the tool), `sliceToolActions`, `sliceTool` (controls, quiet note line, the lazy panel loader with Try again), the crosshair and the two-step tap confirm, the map marks (A, B, the line, the probe's pin), Back and Escape, focus; `e2e/air-tool.spec.ts` | v-tool: pass with fixes (a random walk of 400 runs through the tool's state machine kept its invariants; not committed) | a draft end placed over the Layers panel's own line was hidden on the map until B was placed (fixed); a preset that came out too short said "Make the line longer than 300 m", which nobody can do for a preset, and now says why in words about the fire and the phone (`presetIssueWords`, fixed); the map marks' teal was a literal colour outside the contrast check, now the token `--mark-slice` with its pairs (v-cross, fixed). Left: on the web *Try again* cannot load a chunk that has failed once, a platform limit (17.4); the tool's other findings, fixed in the slice-UI round: after a failed retry *Try again* could not work on the web (the tool now says "Close and reopen FireSim to load it" and puts the button away); a probe stayed on the map after the line was changed from outside the tool, by the compass or *Show me* (it is cleared); a preset tapped from the controls, scrolled down the panel, left the picture above the fold (the tool scrolls to it). Not changed: a tapped candidate beats the crosshair until *Cancel* (the hint says so), and with no fire a preset centred on the map follows the camera at the rate of the snapshots, not at once |
| slicepanel (D, panel) | the side-view panel in one lazy chunk (`slicePanel`, `slicePanelModel`, `sliceDraw`, `slice.css`): the raster or the model's cells, the two grounds, axes, streaks or still arrows, chips with their reasons and the *Use the 3-D atmosphere* button, the legend, the probe with its sentence and keys, the footer, the landscape layout; `e2e/airslice-panel.spec.ts` | v-slice: pass with fixes | the streaks were seeded afresh whenever the line moved by a metre, and a live preset moves with the fire, so playing at 60 x restarted every streak at about 7 updates in 10 (fixed: 69 updates gave 116 clears, now 72 updates give 75); the streaks and the still arrows ran inside the 10 m ground where the model's ground is lower, about 1 % of the valid cells on the Katoomba 6 km slices (fixed); the temperature words said "than the area" where the number is against the slice's own air at that height (fixed, 18.3 item 8). Other findings, fixed in the slice-UI round: the frame-rate cap let a 144 Hz display draw about 29 frames a second (18.3 item 22); a reading of five lines overflowed the panel in short landscape and the pinned reading then covered the whole window (18.3 item 27); `niceTicks` took 2.1 ms of the 7.5 ms redraw at 4x (it counts the candidate steps, builds the winner and keeps the last eight answers); the panel's repaint ran inside the update (18.3 item 18) |
| integration | the seams between the lanes: `e2e/air-slice.spec.ts` (5 tests, both tiers, real touch and real screenshots) and the seam fixes in the next column; its e2e run went from 12 of 14 passing to 14 of 14 | v-cross: pass with fixes | **Seam bugs fixed by the integration lane:** the Layers panel's chips were built unpressed and enabled and only synced at the next slice, so opening Layers after placing a line showed no chip chosen and Temp and Smoke open on the fast tier; the 2-D stand-in map's line drawing was private code that nothing tested (now an exported pure function with tests); in short landscape the reading was cut off under the footer (it takes the footer's line); a control scrolled to stopped under a pinned part (scroll-padding on the panel's body); the controls had no space under them; a blank band of 60 to 100 px stood above a short true-scale picture (the canvas is as tall as its picture); with no line the panel showed empty chips and a legend (the empty state is words and the controls only); six chips did not fit under 412 px (two rows of three); the *Use the 3-D atmosphere* button sat below the fold (it comes before the scene's reasons). **v-cross then fixed:** the chips' accessible names (one constant, `SLICE_THETA_BASIS`), the model card's and the Data sets screen's words for the fast mode (the forecast wind fitted to the terrain, not a 2-D field), the map marks' teal (a token, `--mark-slice`, with contrast pairs), a landscape reading that was still clipped, the Help entry *Air from* (it said the air is usually the time you look at, which is false: 18.3 item 24) and two stale specs. Found and left for the slice-UI round (v-cross): in short landscape with a reading on, the pinned reading covered the whole scroll window, so the chips, *Presets* and the 3-D button could not be reached until the reading was cleared; at 390 x 844 on the fast tier with a reading the chips' second row was cut and the 3-D button was below the fold; at 360 x 640 part of the reading hid under the footer. The covered window and the cut chips are fixed (18.3 item 27); the 3-D button is still a scroll away at 390 x 844 (the controls now come first instead, 18.4), and the hidden reading at 360 x 640 is not fixed, 18.4 |
| history (C, fix round) | the fast tier's wind volume packed in the history: `core/airPack.ts` (`packWind`, `unpackWind`, `windPackErrorBound`: u, v and w as 16-bit integers with a scale for each, 6 bytes per cell, 243 kB at 45 x 45 x 20, an error of at most 0.502 of a step, 0.00046 m/s at 30 m/s); a wind list in `SnapshotStore` with 4 % of the history's room (the embers' 20 % became 16 %; the keyframes keep 72 % and the light frames 8 %); a picture takes the newest saved volume at or before its time and carries that volume's own air time; `keptKeyframeBytesModel` and `windVolumeBytesModel` for the Data sets memory rows; `airPack.test.ts`, `snapshotStore.air.test.ts`, `snapshotStore.fast6h.sim.test.ts`, and changes to `memoryModel.test.ts` and `airView.sim.test.ts` | a fix round on v-airdata's finding F1; no verifier round of its own: the full gate in 18.2 and the e2e specs it touched cover it | none reported; measured and costed in 18.3 item 25 |
| slice-UI (D, fix round) | `render/slice/presetWind.ts` (`presetWindFromDeg`, `PRESET_WIND_AGL_M`); `sectionCompass` and `sameLine` in `render/sliceFields.ts` and the Layers compass; `me` disabled until a fire exists (`hasFire`); `watchLineForProbe`; `loadFailedView`; `flowTick`; the panel's repaint in the next display frame (`catchUp`); cached `niceTicks`; the panel's order and the landscape reading; the fire found once for each snapshot in `SceneView.sliceInputs`; `slice.css` at 4,024 bytes (cap 4,096); `e2e/air-slice.spec.ts`, `airslice-panel.spec.ts` and `air-tool.spec.ts` extended | a fix round on the findings of v-slice, v-tool and v-cross; no verifier round of its own: the full gate in 18.2 and the e2e specs it touched cover it | none reported; what is left is in 18.4 |
| docs | README, ARCHITECTURE, DESIGN, this section; eight Help and Glossary entries and an Air slices how-to step pinned to the code by `content.test.ts`; the screenshot spec for 0.4.0 prepared and not run | v-cross: pass with fixes | figures and sentences corrected in the second docs round, each checked against the code or a command: the regression's worst case is 96.4 %, not 97.6 (a re-run here gave -96.43 % worst and 32.9 % mean); `slice.css` is 3,979 bytes, not 3,780 (`wc -c`); the curtain's arrows read the sampler's `wind()`, not `windP`; the air shown is usually older than the picture; `e2e/air-slice.spec.ts` was missing from three lists (v-cross counted 6 tests in it; `npx playwright test --list` shows 5); the README said one more gallery test where there are two; the temperature wording; the version table (`package.json` is still 0.3.0 until the release); this section's own sentence that the model card still called the fast tier a "2-D surface wind" (fixed in the code by 70c4ed8 after it was written). A third pass, after the history and slice-UI rounds, replaced the markers those rounds left and the sentences they made stale (the +35 MB of history, the preset wind, the 24 frames a second, the landscape and portrait layout, `slice.css` at 4,024 bytes) |

### 18.2 Gate results

All on the final tree (version 0.4.0, versionCode 7), on this build machine (4 cores, software rendering), one run each unless stated:

| Gate | Result |
|---|---|
| `npx tsc --noEmit` | clean |
| `SLOW=1 npx vitest run` (every unit test, including the slow validation suites) | 234 files passed, 6 skipped; 3,880 tests passed, 34 skipped, 1 expected fail (11.7 min). 0.3.0 had 3,331 |
| `npm run build:cap` then `npm run check:bundle` | entry 165.5 kB gzip of 195, all JS 943.3 of 1000, CSS 127.7 kB raw (23.3 gzip) of 135 (25), demo data 33.1 MB of 36; raw JS 2,807 kB, raw entry 478 kB; the four decoders and pako still left out; no budget changed |
| `npm run check:contrast` | ok in the four looks |
| `scripts/layout-budget.mjs` | ok at all five sizes: 390x844 73.9 % (budget 72), 412x915 76.0 (74), 360x740 70.3 (68), 360x640 65.6 (63), 844x390 77.4 (72) |
| `scripts/audit-tap-targets.mjs` | ok on the sim screen (412x915, 844x390, 360x640) and, with the notice accepted, on Setup (390x844 and 360x640). The first Setup run failed only because the audit had not accepted the notice, as in 0.3.0 |
| `npx playwright test` (the whole e2e suite, Pixel 7 profile, real engine) | 138 tests: 135 passed and 3 failed on the first run (58.9 min). The failures: `ui.spec` "the TRAINING strip keeps its full wording" (the console guard saw `net::ERR_INSUFFICIENT_RESOURCES`: the disk had 111 MB free; it failed the same way on the 0.3.0 commit and passes on the final tree after about 7 GB were freed), `airslice-panel.spec` "reduced motion" (a 5 s poll for the picture to change timed out) and `pace.spec` "real engine, slow tier" (the 7 min test timeout). The last two passed when rerun alone (2 passed in 1.8 min); both are slow and load-sensitive specs and I did not find a defect behind either run, so treat them as flaky until a second full run says otherwise. The full suite was not rerun |
| Debug APK | `./gradlew assembleDebug` after `build:cap` and `cap sync android`: `app-debug.apk`, 30,360,109 bytes, 0.4.0, versionCode 7 |

Not run: the phone. Everything the plan says about phones is still `[est]`. The full e2e suite was also not rerun after the disk was freed, only the three failed specs.

### 18.3 Corrections to this plan (what the builds and verifiers proved)

1. 7.2, sampler cost: `windP` takes about 110 ns per call and `fieldsP` (wind, temperature and smoke) about 260 ns, both 0.14 bytes per call, not "about 143 ns" and not the 48 bytes of a boxed call (`[sampler perf]` in `atmosphereSampler.test.ts`). The timings vary from run to run: `windP` took 100 to 130 ns and `fieldsP` 182 to 260 ns in the runs seen. v-airdata found that the first version allocated when V8 did not inline its helper (48 and 80 bytes a call with inlining off) and fixed it; `atmosphereSampler.alloc.test.ts` runs with inlining off. The older entry points `wind()` and `surfaceWind()` take double arguments and box them when not inlined: 62 and 64 bytes a call with inlining off, as they did before 0.4.0, and about 11 kB a frame measured for the standard tier's through-the-plume streaks. Only the slice calls `fieldsP` and nothing in the app calls `windP` yet, so the streaks, the displayed embers and the curtain's arrows still take the boxed path.
2. 7.3, cost of the air view: `view()` takes 0.60 ms and 498 kB in all on the fast tier (45 x 45 x 20), 0.77 ms and 815 kB on the standard tier and 1.16 ms and 1,730 kB on the high tier, not "about 0.5 ms" and "three 162 kB allocations" (`[atmos perf]` in `airView.perf.test.ts`, node, one run; a later run gave 0.40, 0.60 and 1.15 ms for the fast, standard and high tiers and another 0.43 ms (fast) and 1.22 ms (high), so the figures move by a third from run to run). The fast tier's published view carries 474.6 KiB of wind (12 bytes per cell; 486,000 bytes), as the plan said, and the newest snapshot holds it exactly; `keyframeBytesModel` (`scenario/memoryModel.ts`) models it and a test holds it to the real snapshot within 3 %. Kept whole in every history keyframe it would have added about 35 MB over the 72 keyframes of a 6 hour run, and it did what 18.1 says (a keyframe of 1,310,636 B against 824,636 B). It is not kept whole: item 25 supersedes the "+35 MB", and the "+0.49 MB per keyframe" of section 11, for the history.
3. 7.4, gating: the wind streaks "Through the plume" are **not** made unavailable on the fast tier. They stay selectable and show an info note ("These streaks follow the fast model's diagnosed wind (terrain effects only), so there is no plume. Use the 3-D atmosphere to see it."), because the sampler now reads the diagnosed volume. The wind speed heat map is available on the fast tier (it needs a fire, like the other fire maps), its source text says "the forecast wind fitted to the terrain (winds 10 m above the ground)", and `has3dAtmosphere` is asked of the picture's own view (`airKind(view) === 'prognostic'`), as the plan wanted.
4. 7.6, the regression and the oracle: the old flat view reads up to 96.4 % too low at 30 m above a 700 m ridge (the first docs round said 97.6; v-cross re-measured it and a re-run here gave -96.43 %) and 32.9 % too low on average across it (the plan said "more than 30 %", which is the mean), and the oracle's measured worst difference to the engine's own `sample()` is 1.9e-6 m/s on both tiers, against the 1e-3 the tests allow. The oracle covers points up to 2,000 m above the ground, inside the model; above the model top the sampler returns the top level where the engine returns its profile wind with w = 0, which is untested and only the highest arrows and particles could reach.
5. 8.2, the minimum length is the larger of 300 m and two model cells (the plan said both "about 270 m" and 300 m): 300 m on the standard 6 km grid, where two cells are 266 m. The words use the model's own number rounded up to 50 m ("Make the line longer than 300 m").
6. 8.2, `crossSection.field` is optional: absent keeps the old curtain's colouring (temperature where the air has it, else wind speed), so `DEFAULT_LAYERS`, every exact-shape test, `PERSISTED_LAYER_KEYS` and `LAYER_PREFS_VERSION` are untouched; the tool writes `'speed'` with the first line it places and never changes a `field` that exists.
7. 8.2, the line has a precedence the plan left open: a live `preset`, then `a` and `b` both set, then the old chord through `centre` and `azimuth`; a lone `a` or `b` is ignored (the tool keeps a draft until both exist). Whoever writes `centre` or `azimuth` writes `a: undefined, b: undefined, preset: null` (`LINE_BY_CENTRE`); whoever writes `a` and `b` writes `preset: null`.
8. 8.1 and 8.4, temperature: `theta` is the potential-temperature anomaly minus the mean of the valid cells of its own row of the slice ("warmer or cooler than the slice's air at that height"), not "the area average at that height": the slice has no more than its own cells. The words now say so (v-slice fixed the first build's "than the area"): the probe's sentence is "3 K warmer than average" (or "about average temperature" under half a kelvin), the legend's title is "Warmer or cooler than the average of the slice at that height", the chip is named "Air temperature against the average of the slice at that height" (one constant, `SLICE_THETA_BASIS`), and the legend's sentence and the Help say what the average is. **A design point:** because the average is of the line's own air, on a short line through a plume the Temp chip understates the plume, which is part of what it is compared with.
9. 8.4, the stored fields are `speed`, `along`, `across`, `up`, `theta` and `smoke` (the plan's `uAlong`, `uAcross`, `w`), and the signature is `sampleSlice(sampler, terrain, spec, out?)` with `terrain` anything that has `heightAt` (the scene gives it the 10 m DEM as received, never the sharpened cliff drawing). A slice also carries the validity mask, the 10 m ground and the model's ground per column, `topAsl`, `kind`, `airTime`, `shownTime`, a `seq` and the model's own cells.
10. 8.4 and 8.1, "Show model cells" draws `SliceData.cells`, the real model columns and level boundaries that the line crosses, with the model's own value in each, not the sampling lattice at nearest neighbour as the plan had it.
11. 8.4, the up-slope preset is not "DEM aspect from `HeightField`": it is a Sobel gradient of the 10 m ground on a 3 x 3 stencil of 1 km steps (about 2 km across), and ground flatter than 2 degrees has no uphill ("The ground here is too flat to have an uphill direction"). A calm wind (under 0.2 m/s at 10 m) has no direction, so *Along* and *Across* are disabled with "The wind is calm, so it has no direction yet".
12. 8.5, the streaks and the exaggeration: the plan and the contract said to scale the streaks' vertical motion by the on-screen ratio of the picture. The panel gives `stepFlow` the exaggeration 1, because the ratio is already in the drawing (`yOfZ`): giving it as well counts it twice and makes streaks climb `ratio` times steeper than the ground they follow (`slicePanelFlow.test.ts`). `flowArrows`, which draws no ground, is given the ratio. The picture is drawn at the true scale times the existing x1 to 3 slider where that fits in its box, else fitted to the box, and the tag says the ratio really drawn ("True scale", "Height ×1.5").
13. 8.5, colours: speed uses `WIND_RAMP` (0 to 25 m/s), up and down `UPDRAFT_RAMP` (-4 to +8), temperature `THETA_RAMP` (-10 to +20 K), along and across the new symmetric `ALONG_RAMP`, and **smoke is a log ramp**, `SMOKE_RAMP`, four decades from 0.0001 to 1 (the plan named no ramp for it): the smoke of a young plume is about 0.0005 to 0.001 of "full" after 5 to 15 minutes of a small fire and a linear scale shows nothing (v-tool saw the smoke through a plume peak at about 1e-3, a pale chip: honest). Colour-vision measurements are in `docs/DESIGN.md`; the older temperature and up/down ramps pass with floors of 8 and 6 (measured 8.9 under protan vision and 6.4 under deutan), below the 9 the heat-map ramps keep, and were covered by the tests for the first time.
14. 8.5 and 11, lazy loading: the panel, its drawing, `flow2d`, `probe`, `niceTicks`, the lattice reader and `slice.css` are one dynamic chunk; the tool's shell, its model and its actions are in the entry, and `sampleSlice`, the updater and `sectionLine` go with the scene (the render chunk, and `sectionLine` is also imported by the 2-D mock, so the build may share it) because the scene computes the slice for the curtain too (the plan put `sliceField` in the panel's chunk). Probe's use of `compassWord`, `int` and `short` from `explain/text.ts` makes the build share that file as a **chunk of its own** (a lane reported about 12 kB gzip), which counts in all JS. Sizes in the release build: the panel chunk 10.5 kB gzip (26.1 raw) plus 0.9 kB of CSS, the `explain/text` chunk 12.1 kB (35.5 raw), the `sectionLine` chunk 3.7 kB (9.0 raw), the entry 165.5 kB against 159.7 in 0.3.0 (+5.8), all JavaScript 943.3 kB against 912.5 (+30.8). The plan estimated 14 to 18 kB for the panel's chunk. The budgets were not touched.
15. 8.1, 8.5 and 8.6, words: the wind is "from the north-west" (hyphenated, the app's own `compassWord`), not "northwest"; the footer reads "3-D time-stepped air · model grid 133 m · air from 11:25" or "Diagnosed wind: terrain effects only · model grid 133 m · air from 11:25", with " · " between the parts, "(N min before the time shown)" past 90 s, and "air time not recorded" for a view without a time.
16. 8.3, the panel does not own the placement buttons: the tool builds them and the panel places them, right after the chips, before the notes of the chips that are off and (on the fast tier) the legend (item 27); the first build put them between the legend and the footer. A tap on the map while placing sets a dashed mark and *Place A here* keeps it (the two-step confirm); `'air'` was added to `ToolId` together with its factory; a map tap and the crosshair work while one finger still pans.
17. 8.6, the curtain's texture is now the slice's own lattice (up to 192 x 56, one texel per lattice point, row 0 lowest), not its own 128 x 48 lattice rebuilt on every update; its ground line is the 10 m terrain as received, so a cliff the 3-D view draws steeper shows at its data slope in the curtain. Its arrows still read the sampler, and the cyan reverse-flow arrows are drawn on the prognostic air only.
18. 8.8, performance: one slice update (resolve, four preset checks, sample, cells) takes a median of 0.5 to 1.0 ms and a 95th percentile of up to 1.5 ms in node, unthrottled, on 96 x 55 and 129 x 55 lattices (up to 1.59 ms in another run). The plan's limit is 8 ms at 4x CPU throttle, and three verifiers then measured it in a real browser in software rendering under a 4x throttle (not on a phone): resolve plus sample took 2.5 ms per edit, the panel's redraw 7.5 ms and the curtain 1.2 ms (v-slice); the compute alone had a 95th percentile of 7.1 ms on the fast tier and 8.9 ms on the 3-D tier (v-cross), so the compute is borderline; the whole update through `setLayers` had a median of 12.4 ms and a 95th percentile of 22.7 ms against 2.8 and 7 ms unthrottled (v-slice), a 95th percentile of 18 ms on the fast tier and 33 ms on the 3-D tier with a maximum of 41 ms (v-cross), and 19.6 to 24.4 ms (fast) and 21 to 24.7 ms (3-D) with the panel open against 5.9 to 10.4 ms with it closed (v-tool: the panel's repaint, done inside each update, added about 14 ms at 4x, and updates come at most four a second while playing). So the limit was not met for the whole update, and the repaint was then changed (item 27). Measured again after the slice-UI round, in the same kind of browser (software rendering, 4x throttle, the fast tier with the panel open, 150 line edits, three runs, not a phone): the synchronous update took a median of 4.2 to 4.5 ms and a 95th percentile of 7.9 to 9.5 ms (it was 10.5 to 11.5 and 17.9 to 20.9 in the same harness), and the panel's repaint, now a frame of its own, a median of 3.3 to 4.4 ms and a 95th percentile of 8.4 to 10.4; update plus repaint is a 95th percentile of about 15 to 17 ms (it was 24 to 26). The repaint was moved, not removed: the plan's 8 ms is approached and not met for the whole update, and the 95th percentile of the synchronous part alone straddles it. The 3-D tier was measured once: 5.9 ms median and 12.6 ms 95th percentile of the synchronous update, against 12.7 and 22.3 before. 3,000 updates in a row leave the heap flat (it changed by 31.8 kB after a collection, 31.6 kB in a second run), and so do 60 opens and closes of the panel in the browser (v-slice). One long task of 1.76 s was seen once in a fast-tier slice window and did not reproduce (v-cross).
19. 7.7 and 13, documents: README, ARCHITECTURE and DESIGN are updated, with the release build's figures. Not updated, because research is not edited: the passages of `docs/research/00-synthesis.md` that describe the old view (see 18.4). The model card's words that called the fast tier "a 2-D surface wind" (true of what the fire feels, incomplete about what is published) were fixed in the code after the first docs round (70c4ed8: "the forecast wind fitted to the terrain", and the fire "feels a 2-D surface wind built from it"). `e2e/app.spec.ts` was changed so that the section's own note and button (`slice-diagnosed`, `use-3d-slice`) replace `need-3d` and `use-3d` for the slice, and `robustness.spec.ts`'s comment about the wind speed map now says both tiers publish a wind.
20. 8.9 and 13, screenshots: 05 (the curtain) and 09 (the Tools dial, which gains Air slices) are stale and `docs/screenshots/design/app-sim-menus-*.png` too; `e2e/gallery.spec.ts` has two `gallery 0.4.0` tests for the four new pictures (33, 34 and 35 in the first, 34b in the second). None of this was run by the docs lane.
21. 8.9, the ridge-crest acceptance (v-slice, real app, one ridge, one run): the probe 30 m above the ground read 45 km/h from the north-west where the model's own 10 m wind was 34.6 km/h and the forecast about 33, that is x1.30 and x1.36. A log law with a roughness length of 0.5 m gives x1.37 from 10 m to 30 m (computed here; the verifier gave 1.37 without naming its roughness), so the slice is plausible against the surface wind, as the acceptance asked.
22. 8.5, the 24 frames a second cap: its threshold is 1/24 s minus 8 ms, so what is drawn follows the display: about 20 frames a second at 60 Hz, 24 at 120 Hz and 29 at 144 Hz (v-slice). README and ARCHITECTURE said "at most 24", which was true of 60 and 120 Hz only. The slice-UI round changed the threshold to 1/24 s minus half a millisecond (`flowTick` in `slicePanelModel.ts`): at most 24 frames a second on any display, 20 at 60 Hz and 24 at 120 and 144 Hz (`slicePanelModel.test.ts` runs 12 display rates from 30 to 360 Hz, and three with jitter), so "at most 24" is now true everywhere.
23. 7.4 and 13, the fast tier's words: the tier keeps its name "Fast (surface wind)" (the fire does feel a 2-D surface wind built from the diagnosed volume), but what it publishes is now called what it is. The Wind streaks entry says "the fast mode's diagnosed wind: the forecast wind fitted to the terrain ... (terrain effects only, so there is no plume in it)", its grid is "133 m grid of wind cells (the diagnosed wind)" (it said "surface wind only") and its "How it is drawn" is a 3-D volume on both tiers (it said 3-D objects on the fast tier); the smoke stays 3-D objects there. The wind speed map's legend, on either tier, still has one title, "Wind speed near the ground", because the legend is not told the tier (plan 7.4 had asked the map to say "10 m wind from the terrain fit"); its note and its Layers text now say it is the model's 10 m wind fitted to the terrain, and that on a calm night the wind the fire feels can differ a lot from it (18.4).
24. 8.6, "air from": the plan said scrubbing back composes the keyframe at or before the time, up to 300 s older. That is also what happens while a run plays: only the newest picture carries air made at its own time, every earlier picture takes the saved air at or before it (kept about every 300 s where the history has room: a 2 GB phone, for a 6 hour run), so the footer says, for example, "(4 min before the time shown)" while playing at 60 x (2 to 5 minutes, v-cross), not only after a jump back. On a 0.5 or 1 GB phone the fast tier's saved wind is thinned to a share of the history (item 25), and the air of an old picture of a 6 hour run can be hours older than the picture; the footer says so in minutes.
25. 7.3 and 11, the history's memory (fix F1; this supersedes the "+35 MB" of item 2 and the "+0.49 MB per keyframe, about +35 MB" of section 11 for the history): the fast tier's wind volume is kept packed. `core/airPack.ts` stores each of u, v and w as signed 16-bit integers with a scale of its own (`q = round(f / s)`, `s = max|f| / 32767`), 6 bytes per cell, 243 kB at 45 x 45 x 20; every restored value is within 0.502 of a step of the exact one, that is `0.502 * max|f| / 32767` per field, 0.00046 m/s at 30 m/s (on the real Katoomba view, with largest |u|, |v| and |w| of 15.0, 14.2 and 6.2 m/s, the worst differences were 2.3e-4, 2.2e-4 and 9.4e-5 m/s, at the bound). `SnapshotStore` keeps the volumes in a list of their own with 4 % of the room the history has (the embers' share went from 20 % to 16 %; the keyframes keep 72 % and the light frames 8 %), so a volume never costs a picture. Until the budget is first reached every keyframe keeps its volume; after that the list is held to its share at every step and thinned by age like the others, and thinning only the wind does not count in `thinnedCount`. A picture takes the newest saved volume at or before its time and carries that volume's own air time, so the footer's "N min before the time shown" stays true; the live view, the newest snapshot, the engine and the 3-D tiers' views are exact. Measured with `npx vitest run src/ui/snapshotStore.fast6h.sim.test.ts --reporter=verbose --silent=false` (real Katoomba fast-tier snapshots, 40,000 fire cells, 6 h, step 60 s), keyframes kept (longest gap in minutes) at the 6 hour instant at 48 / 61.44 / 64 / 122.88 MiB: 0.3.0 35 (40) / 55 (10) / 53 (20) / 72 (5); before the fix 22 (40) / 33 (40) / 31 (40) / 72 (5); now 39 (20) / 57 (10) / 55 (10) / 72 (5). The count is a sawtooth, so one instant is partly luck; over the last hour the mean count is about 5 % lower than 0.3.0's. The mean keyframe with its volume weighs about 868 kB (+5 %; 0.3.0 824,636 B, the unfixed 1,310,636 B). At 2 GB nothing is thinned: every keyframe has its volume (72 volumes, 17.5 MB). The price is the freshness of old air: the air of an old picture of a 6 hour run can be hours older than the picture (up to 239 / 119 / 129 min at 48 / 61.4 / 64 MiB) and the footer says so in minutes. Not measured: any of this on a phone.
26. 8.3 and 8.4, the presets' wind: the plan took the wind direction from the forecast at the shown time. *Along* and *Across* now follow the model's own wind at the middle of the preset line (the fire, else the map centre), `PRESET_WIND_AGL_M` = 30 m above the 10 m ground there (`presetWindFromDeg`, `render/slice/presetWind.ts`), because that is the air the slice shows; the forecast's 10 m direction is only the fallback, for an air with no 3-D wind or a model wind there under 0.2 m/s. *Me to fire* is disabled, with "There is no fire to go to yet", until a fire exists; the tool finds the fire with the scene's rule (`fireCentroid`) once for each snapshot, and so does `SceneView.sliceInputs`. `e2e/air-slice.spec.ts` holds each preset to the model's wind read from the slice's own lattice.
27. 8.3 and 8.5, the panel, the Layers compass and the tool after the slice-UI round. **Repaint:** `setData` only notes the slice; the layout, words, picture and probe reading are redone in the next display frame, once however many slices came, and nothing is drawn while the panel is covered (item 18 has the cost). **Order and reach:** portrait is the picture, the reading, the chips, the tool's controls, the notes of the chips that are off with the 3-D button, then the legend (on the 3-D air the legend comes before the controls); at 390 x 844 *Set start* and *Presets* are in view and the 3-D button is a scroll away (a tap on a chip that is off scrolls to it), at 360 x 640 only the picture and the chips fit in the window of about 230 px; under 412 px the chips are two rows of three. In short landscape the picture (84 px, as wide as the panel) and a one-line footer are pinned and the reading scrolls with the chips and controls between them, so with a reading on the chips, *Presets* and the 3-D button can be reached and tapped; the footer gives its line to the reading while there is one. **Layers:** the chips are headed *Colour the slice by*, the compass is *Slice direction* and shows the placed line's own bearing, turning it swings that line about its middle (out to the model's edges; the preset or A and B are cleared), *Centre on the fire* moves it to the fire at the same bearing, and a hint above says so. **Tool:** a failed retry says "The side view could not be loaded. Close and reopen FireSim to load it." and shows no button; a probe is cleared when the line is changed from outside the tool. `slice.css` is 4,024 bytes (cap 4,096). The plan's 8.3 and the first contract text said the panel places the controls between the legend and the footer: item 16.

### 18.4 Known limitations and what is left

- **Nothing about phones is measured:** the panel's feel under a finger, the streaks' frame rate, the chunk's load time, the history's thinning, the memory at the peak. The slice update was measured at a 4x CPU throttle in a desktop browser in software rendering (18.3 item 18), which is not a phone. The verifiers did not check TalkBack, forced colours, a calm wind on the real app, or the tool in landscape (844 x 390) in a picture (the slice-UI round took landscape pictures of the panel, in software rendering), and the cyan reverse-flow arrows and the grey rule for smoke never appeared in a picture they took (they rest on unit tests). Phone check-list 2 (section 12) is the way to find out; copy the statistics afterwards.
- **The wind a slice and the wind speed map show is the air's published 10 m wind, not always the wind the fire feels; on a calm night they differ a lot, on both tiers.** On the fast tier a slice shows the forecast wind fitted to the terrain and nothing else (no night-time decoupling, slope flows, lee blend or fire indraft, no plume, temperature or smoke). The footer, the legend and the note say so, and "air from" says when. v-airdata measured the gap on Katoomba (a 6 km area, 4,096 cells) as the ratio of the wind the fire feels to the published 10 m wind: on a windy day or night (7 to 10 m/s) the median is about 1.0 and the 10th to 90th percentiles are 0.81 to 1.10; on the calm-night preset on the fast tier the 10th percentile is 0.35 and the 90th 1.91, with 16.6 % of the cells under half and 20.5 % over one and a half times; on the standard tier 31.9 % of the cells are over one and a half times. The plan's estimate in 7.3, that at night or in a valley the fire feels at most 30 % of the forecast wind, is close to the 10th percentile there. The docs round re-ran the comparison with a throwaway test that was not committed (Katoomba 6 km, fast tier, one hour into the run, every seventh cell of the 200 x 200 fire grid): on the calm-night preset the median was 1.05, the 10th and 90th percentiles 0.34 and 2.22, with 17.9 % under half and 24.8 % over one and a half times; on the hot north-westerly preset at 11:00 the median was 1.00 and the 10th and 90th percentiles 0.88 and 1.05. That is the same picture within the spread of the sampling, and it fixes which way the ratio goes. The wind speed map's Layers text, its legend's note and its Glossary entry say so, and so does the Help entry *Diagnosed wind* for the fast tier. The measure was not taken on the other presets, on other sites or with the fire's own wind near a burning front.
- **Temperature is against the slice's own air.** The words were fixed (18.3 item 8: "3 K warmer than average", "the average of the slice at that height"), but the design stays: on a short line through a plume the Temp chip understates the plume, because the plume is part of the air it is compared with. A longer line, or a reference taken from the model's whole level, would understate it less; neither is built.
- The map marks of the tool use the token `--mark-slice` (`#00796b`), which `check:contrast` now checks: 5.32:1 against its white halo, and the white 19 px bold letter on the teal disc as large text. The contrast against the aerial photo behind the halo was not audited: the halo carries the edge there.
- Layout, as measured by the slice-UI round in software rendering (a log of element positions at 390 x 844, 360 x 640 and 844 x 390, and pictures; not a phone): in short landscape the picture and the footer are pinned and the reading, the chips and the controls scroll between them, so with a reading on the chips, *Presets* and the 3-D button can be reached; the window they scroll in is about 68 px with a line and no reading and about 97 px with one (the footer gives its line to the reading). In portrait the controls come right after the chips: at 390 x 844 *Set start* and *Presets* are in view and the *Use the 3-D atmosphere* button is a scroll away (it was in view before the controls were moved up; a tap on a chip that is off scrolls to it); at 360 x 640 only the picture and the chips fit in the window of about 230 px. Left as it is: at 360 x 640 a reading of five lines is about 112 px where 80 to 90 px are left under the picture, so it needs a scroll to read in full; the Tools dial scrolls at 360 x 640 (as the builders reported, not re-checked). The blank band above a long line at true scale was fixed in integration (the canvas is as tall as its picture). `scripts/layout-budget.mjs` and `scripts/audit-tap-targets.mjs` were run in the slice-UI round on the app's own sim screen and Setup (exit 0 each; they do not open the slice panel), so for the panel itself they, and the Layers panel's DOM node count with the section switched on (940 against a limit of 1,000 at 0.3.0, before the chip row existed), are still for the gates.
- The slice-UI round (what the verifiers found in the presets, the compass, the layout, the frame-rate cap and the panel's repaint) is in 18.1 and in 18.3 items 18, 22, 26 and 27. What it left: the plan's 8 ms at a 4x throttle is approached and not met for the whole update (item 18); two small findings of v-tool were not changed (a tapped candidate beats the crosshair until *Cancel*, which the hint says, and with no fire a preset centred on the map follows the camera at the rate of the snapshots); the Layers panel's diagnosed-wind note still begins "This section shows"; and the layout leftovers above.
- The history of a small phone (fix F1, 18.3 item 25): the fast tier's wind volume is kept packed (16-bit integers, 6 bytes per cell) in a list with 4 % of the history's room, so a 6 hour run keeps about the pictures 0.3.0 kept: 39 / 57 / 55 keyframes at 48 / 61.4 / 64 MiB against 0.3.0's 35 / 55 / 53 at the 6 hour instant (the mean over the last hour is about 5 % lower; the longest gap at that instant is the same or shorter: 20 / 10 / 10 min against 40 / 10 / 20). The price is the freshness of the air in old pictures, which can be hours older than the picture (up to 239 / 119 / 129 min at those budgets; the footer says so), and a wind that differs from the exact one by at most 0.00046 m/s at 30 m/s. Measured in node on real fast-tier snapshots (`snapshotStore.fast6h.sim.test.ts`); nothing about it was measured on a phone.
- Smoke puffs: v-airdata counted the puffs whose centre lies below the 10 m ground, 5.9 % of them against 4.8 % in 0.3.0, and those within a quarter of their radius of it, 20.7 % against 13.9 %. Mild, and not changed.
- The Map view tab, the horizontal wind slice and the glyphs are 0.5.0; the time-lapse recorder is 0.4.1 (nothing defines `SliceRecorder`; the five truncate sites of `session.ts` that the plan names are untouched).
- 0.3.1: H-sim and the speed test button, waiting for the S6 re-label. The open owner questions of 17.4 (the in-thread engine and the demo engine in the APK; the mask's and the mesh's CPU budgets) stand.
- The slice and the wind speed map read the 10 m terrain as received; the sharpened cliff drawing never reaches a number.
- Words left as they were: the tier is still called "Fast (surface wind)" in the debug statistics and the model card, "simple 2-D surface wind" in Setup's hint, ", simple 2-D wind" in Settings and "Fast surface-wind model" in the engine's reason text (all true of what the fire feels; the tier also publishes the diagnosed 3-D wind); the Layers panel now says "slice" (its chips, its compass and its announcement) except for the diagnosed-wind note, which still begins "This section shows the diagnosed wind".
- `docs/research/00-synthesis.md` is research and was not edited. These lines still describe the old flat view and are wrong for 0.4.0: 372 (`view()` "flat levels"), 418 to 421 (`view()` resamples onto flat levels, "zero below the terrain", "meaning kept"), 1499 ("flat levels above z_min"), 1671 (the fast tier has "a zero 3-D volume") and 2150 (the t0 snapshot has "no 3-D volume"). Where they disagree with section 7, section 18 and the code, those win.

### 18.5 Decisions taken for you in 0.4.0 (change any of them)

The existing bundle budget is reused (you said so): entry 195 kB and all JS 1000 kB gzip are unchanged and no check was weakened, so the panel is a lazy chunk, and the figures are in `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` once the release build fills them in. 0.4.0 was chosen as the next version over 0.3.1, because H-sim waits for the S6 re-label of the aerial tiles by someone who knows the escarpments (D17). The fast tier shows its diagnosed wind in a slice, labelled as such, rather than turning the tool off. `crossSection.field` is optional and absent keeps the old colouring (6). The shortest line is the larger of 300 m and two model cells (5). Temperature is against the slice's own air at each height (8). "Show model cells" draws the model's real cells (10). The history keeps an older picture's fast-tier wind packed to 16 bits in a list of its own with 4 % of its room (not whole, and not at half resolution), so a small phone keeps about the pictures 0.3.0 kept and the air of its old pictures is older (item 25). The Along and Across presets follow the model's wind, not the forecast's (item 26). The in-thread engine and the demo engine stay in the APK (D10 default, as in 0.3.0). The version in `package.json` and `android/app/build.gradle` stays 0.3.0 (versionCode 6) until the release is cut, so a debug build of this tree still says 0.3.0. Commit trailers carry no model name.


## 19. 0.4.1 as built (2026-10-16)

**Status.** The time-lapse recorder for a pinned Air slice line (8.6, decision D7) is built and merged on the designated branch, and checked by two independent verifiers on the real app after the merge (v-ui the words, the tool and the panel, v-core the recorder, the invalidation and the cost); one fix round followed them, and what each did about a finding is written in the same row, in 19.3 and in 19.4. It is the only change of the release: no fire, fuel, terrain or air code changed, the history (`ui/snapshotStore.ts`) has no diff, and the bundle budget is unchanged (entry 195 kB, all JS 1000 kB gzip). versionCode 8; 0.5.0 becomes versionCode 9. All of it ran on software rendering and in node: **nothing has been seen on a phone**. The gate results are in 19.2. E (the horizontal wind slice and glyphs, 0.5.0) and H-sim (0.3.1) are not in 0.4.1. Where 19.3 contradicts section 8.6, the design file or 18.4, 19.3 wins.

### 19.3 Corrections to the plan and the design (what the builds and verifiers proved)

1. 8.6, where it hooks in: the session is `src/ui/session.ts`, not `src/core`; the five truncate sites and `onSnapshot` are now at `session.ts` 458 (`prepareEditAt`), 509 (`rerunWith`), 530 (`rerunFromView`, called by `setSolverStep`), 553 (`removeIgnition`) and 612 (`onRewound`), with `onSnapshot`'s feed at 623 (the plan's 436, 487, 508, 531, 588 and 596 were of an older tree). All five call one private helper, `cutHistory(t)`, which does `snapshots.truncateAfter(t)` and `view.sliceRecording?.truncateAfter(t)`; a source-audit test holds `session.ts` to exactly one `snapshots.truncate` call, inside it.
2. 8.6, the size of a sample and the worst lattice: the plan's "5 fields at 80 x 32 is about 25 KB" is too small. A lattice is 48 to 192 columns (one for each half model cell of the line) by 48 to 56 rows, and a 6 km line on the 133 m grid is 90 x 54 = 4,860 points: 48,696 B a sample on the 3-D air, 29,256 B on the fast tier (3 fields), with the epoch's 5,772 B; 258 and 429 samples fit in 12 MiB. The design file then sized the worst case at 192 x 56 (107,616 B, 116 samples). **It cannot occur: the longest lattice is 127 x 56** (170 x 56 on the High tier), because the model cell is `clamp(extent / nTier, 100, 270)` m, the longest line is the model's diagonal and a lattice has one column for each half cell, so the columns are at most `2 sqrt(2) nTier` whatever the area is (3, 6 or 9 km): 71,216 B a sample on the 3-D air and 42,768 B on the fast tier (95,296 and 57,216 B on the High tier), 176 and 294 samples (131 and 219) in 12 MiB. The perf test keeps 192 x 56 as a harsher bound (its `arrive` + sample + store is 2.18 ms p95, over the design's 1.5 ms and inside the test's 3 ms).
3. 8.6, "at every display step": the picture on screen is `store.at(viewTime, quantum)`, composed and rounded (`playQuantum`), so only `SimSession.onSnapshot` sees every picture the worker makes; the recorder is fed there and not from `SceneView.update`.
4. 8.6, "pinned line": the plan did not say that a 0.4.0 preset is live (18.3 item 26: it follows the model's wind and the fire), so it cannot be recorded, and neither can the Layers chord. Only a hand-placed line (A and B) is. *Keep this line* freezes the preset in force into a hand-placed one (the `swapEnds` pattern: A and B written from the resolved line, `preset: null`, the probe cleared) and the status line says why a preset is not recorded and how to record it.
5. The look-ahead: the worker computes `max(3 x step, 30 x speed, 1800)` s ahead of the view (`runTarget`), so a line placed while playing is recorded from where the worker had got to, 1,800 s ahead at 60 x and 3,600 s at 120 x, which is after the time shown. That is not a bug of the recorder, and it is why the status line says "Recorded from 13:06, as far ahead as the simulation had run" for a time before it. No retroactive fill was built (19.5).
6. 8.6, the names: the plan's `uAlong`, `uAcross` and `w` are `along`, `across` and `up` (18.3 item 9). The recorder stores along, across and up, and theta and smoke on the 3-D air; `speed` is not stored but derived (`hypot(along, across)`: exactly the horizontal speed, because along and across are the wind turned by a unit vector). Smoke is a log code (a linear one would put the 1e-4 end of the legend on three steps): within a relative 3.3e-4 from 1e-6 to 1e3, and 0 below 1e-6. Every other field is within 0.502 of a step, 0.00046 m/s at 30 m/s.
7. 8.6, "tier switches invalidate": a tier switch is not a session site. The worker restores a checkpoint, says `rewound` (which is the fifth site) and sends every picture after it again, so the first picture re-emitted drops the stale samples by time order (`SnapshotStore.push` and `SliceRecorder.arrive` both cut at the incoming time). The samples carry their `kind` and the epoch its lattice, so the recording crosses a switch between the standard and the fast tier (they are built on one atmosphere grid: `ATMOS_TIERS` has the same `nTier` and levels for both; *Use the 3-D atmosphere* asks for the standard tier) and `read` never mixes the two kinds on screen. The High tier has a finer grid, so a switch to it starts another epoch and drops the old recording ("The air model changed, so the earlier recording was dropped.").
8. The design's thinning (D-CONTRACT 2.4), two corrections found by the builder and by v-core. (a) The newest sample, when halving would drop it, **replaces the last one kept** instead of being added after it, because the design's variant left a closer pair that a later thinning kept in the middle of the list (the contract's own "spacing at least the gap" test failed), and `spread()` removes the later of any pair the halving left closer than the new gap (the earlier when the later is the newest). (b) The first gap is twice the **median** spacing of the stored pictures, not the least: the least let a picture a few seconds after another (the run-end picture of a pause) set it, and the status said "about every 5 s" of pictures 2 minutes apart for as long as the recording lasted, because every later thinning doubles the gap and the spacing alike. The first and the newest picture always stay, and no two are closer than the gap.
9. The contract types of the C0 commit lacked `setHistoryBudget?(historyBytes)`: `SliceRecordingApi` gained it as an additive optional member, which the session calls at construction (`view.sliceRecording?.setHistoryBudget?.(this.snapshots.maxBytes)`), so the recorder's budget follows the history's.
10. The words (the design's 4.2) changed after v-ui: "Recording starts with the next picture" is "Recording starts when the simulation next runs."; a time before the first picture says "Recorded from 13:06, as far ahead as the simulation had run. This time is earlier, so it uses the history's saved air, which can be older."; the spacing is in brackets ("Recorded from 11:30 to 12:40 (every picture kept)." and "(about every 2 min)."); "Recording cleared." is dropped once the recording holds a picture again (a drop for another reason keeps its sentence); the announcements are "Line kept. Recording starts when the simulation next runs." and "Recording cleared. Recording starts when the simulation next runs."; the statistics row's value while waiting is "Waiting for the simulation to run" (the design: "Waiting for the next picture"). Every sentence is pinned in `recordingWords.test.ts` and `slicePanelModel.test.ts`.
11. 8.6 and 11, the budget: not "within a 12 MB budget" but `sliceRecorderBudgetBytes(historyBytes)` = `min(12 MiB, round(historyBytes / 8))`: 6 MiB at the history's 48 MiB floor, 7.68 MiB at 61.44, 12 MiB from 96 MiB (a phone of about 1.6 GB), **on top of** the history, which is not changed at all (`snapshotStore.ts` has no diff; `memoryModel.test.ts` holds the `snapshots` item and the totals at 48, 61.44 and 122.88 MiB and reads the source for any mention of the recorder). The Data sets memory model gets one appended note ("An Air slices recording adds up to 6.3 MB while a line is recorded; it is not in the figures above." for a 48 MiB history, 12.6 MB from 96 MiB) and no row. The statistics row counts live samples only: the recorder also keeps a free list of the arrays of dropped samples (a verifier saw 12.4 MB held while the row said 6.7 MB), held to what the budget has room for, so the recording is never over its budget.
12. Focus (the design said nothing): after *Keep this line* or *Clear recording* the pressed button is put away, and the answer is the status line, which sits a screen below the controls on a phone; focus first fell on *Set start (A)*, which says nothing. It now goes to the status line, a `.hint` paragraph with `tabindex="-1"` (not a stop of the Tab key) that scrolls into view clear of the pinned footer; *Set start (A)* is the fallback while the panel has no status line.
13. "No new CSS: `slice.css` is 4,024 of 4,096 bytes" (design 5): one rule changed, the margin of the tool's button rows from 2 px to 4 px (at 2 px the 44 px hit areas of *Presets* and the new row overlapped and `audit-tap-targets.mjs` flagged "Presets misses 1/8"), and `slice.css` is **4,066 bytes** (30 left).
14. Cost: the design's limit (`arrive` + sample + `store` at most 1.5 ms p95 in node at 90 x 54) holds: 1.23 ms on the 3-D air and 0.71 ms on the fast tier (a docs-lane run of `sliceRecorder.perf.test.ts`; the builder's was 1.26). The whole `record(snapshot)` path at 96 x 56 is a median of 1.00 ms and a p95 of 1.31 ms. In a real browser in software rendering under a 4x CPU throttle at Max speed on a 6 km line (a verifier; not a phone), `record()` took a median of 3.8 ms, a p95 of 13.2 ms and a maximum of 22 ms on the fast tier (14 pictures arrive each second, a mean 6.7 % of wall time, the worst second 132 ms) and 4.7, 11.1 and 29 ms on the 3-D tier (5 a second, 2.7 %): highest in the first 20 to 50 seconds after *Keep this line*, falling as thinning halves the rate at which pictures are stored, and **a single call can exceed the 16.7 ms of a 60 frames-a-second frame**. With the slice off, or a preset, a call costs 0.03 to 0.1 ms and stores nothing. If frame drops show up on a phone: sample in idle time, or cap the pictures stored per wall second (neither is built).
15. Chunks (design 7, risk 7): `lattice.ts` became a shared chunk (0.7 kB gzip) because the curtain in the render chunk and the panel both read it, and `core/sliceRecording.ts` (0.8 kB; its budget functions are used by the entry's memory model and model card) and `ui/recordingWords.ts` (0.8 kB; shared by the statistics chunk and the panel) did too. The entry grew by 0.5 kB gzip (165.5 to 166.0) and all JS by 6.0 kB (943.3 to 949.3); the panel chunk is 10.3 kB gzip.
16. Not the recorder's: 18.5 said the version "stays 0.3.0 (versionCode 6) until the release is cut"; 0.4.0 was cut (`2d65e0e`), `package.json` and `android/app/build.gradle` say 0.4.0 (versionCode 7), and the README's version table now says so. 0.4.1's own version bump is the orchestrator's: until it is cut they still say 0.4.0.

### 19.4 Known limitations and what is left

- **Nothing about phones is measured.** The recorder's cost at Max speed (19.3 item 14) is a main-thread load of the first minute after *Keep this line*, measured at a 4x throttle in a desktop browser in software rendering; the memory it adds (6 to 12 MiB on top of the history) is a byte model and node counts; the statistics screen's new row and phone check-list 2 are the way to find out (section 12).
- **A line placed while the run plays is recorded only from where the simulation had computed to** (19.3 item 5), which can be well after the time shown, and nothing is filled in backwards. The status line says where the recording starts. Filling backwards (a worker rewind that changes nothing and re-emits every picture) was not built: it would cost up to the look-ahead of computing, add code and a new cancel case, and change no result (19.5).
- **F2 (v-ui, a nit, decided and not fixed):** in short landscape (844 x 390) with a probe on, the footer is hidden by the 0.4.0 rule (the reading takes its line), so nothing in view says "recorded" or how old the air is while probing. Keeping the footer would clip the reading, and a word added to the reading would lengthen it at 360 x 640, where it already wraps to five lines; at 360 x 640 in portrait the footer stays in view. The probe sentence and its announcement never say "recorded". The gate's tap audit at 844 x 390 passes only because the mock has no slice; with the real engine the same audit reports 3 of 8 probes missed for every control of the tool (probably the pinned picture covering the upper probes after the audit's centred scroll; not investigated, and 0.4.0 has the same layout).
- Not seen on screen: the thinned wording "about every N min" needs about 4 hours of run at a 60 s step to appear (258 samples; the real worst lattice 176) and is unit-tested only, and so is the "Recording stopped" state (a recorder that failed).
- `record()` takes the line's key from the picture's atmosphere grid and `follow()` from the resolved line of the scene, which could flap for a line clipped by the model's edge when one session had pictures with two different grids; that is real in the code and not reachable in the shipped UI.
- *Clear recording* has no undo. A recorded slice has no model cells (*Show model cells* says so and draws the smooth picture). A switch to the High tier drops the recording; one between the standard and the fast tier keeps it.
- The statistics row counts live samples only (the free list is not in it); the Data sets memory model's note is the only place the recorder is in that model, and it is not in its totals.
- TalkBack may read the focused status line and the polite announcement twice after a press (not checked); forced colours and the phone were not checked either.
- e2e: "real engine: Settings changed mid-run" (`robustness.spec.ts`) failed on 0.4.0 too under load: the engine needs about 5 to 6 s to reach 300 s of run time and the poll's default is 5 s; the spec now allows 30 s (`5524640`). The other known items of 18.4 stand (the 8 ms at a 4x throttle for the whole slice update, the layout leftovers at 360 x 640, the words left as they were, `docs/research/00-synthesis.md` not edited).
- 0.3.1 (H-sim and the speed test button) still waits for the S6 re-label; the Map view tab, the horizontal wind slice and the glyphs are 0.5.0 (versionCode 9).

### 19.5 Decisions taken for you in 0.4.1 (change any of them)

The recorder's memory comes **on top of** the history (6 to 12 MiB, an eighth of the history's budget, at most 12 MiB); the history keeps what it kept in 0.4.0. Had the 12 MiB come out of the history, the design scout's synthetic replay of the real `SnapshotStore` (a 6 h run at a 60 s step, 40,000 fire cells; not run again) gave the mean number of keyframes kept over the last hour as 44.5 down to 29.0 on the fast tier at a 48 MiB history (-35 %) and 28.5 down to 19.5 on the standard tier at 61.4 MiB (-32 %), so a 0.5 to 1 GB phone would keep 16 to 35 % fewer pictures; change it by giving the recorder a share of `defaultSnapshotBudget` in `session.ts` instead of an addition. There is **no retroactive replay**: a line placed while playing is recorded from the worker's head onward (the other choice: placing a line also re-runs from the time shown, so the recording starts there). **0.4.1 was chosen as the next increment** over 0.3.1, which waits for the S6 re-label of the aerial tiles (D17), and over 0.5.0. After *Keep this line* or *Clear recording* **focus goes to the status line**, not to *Set start (A)*. **F2 is left:** nothing in view says "recorded" while a probe is shown in short landscape. Decided in the design and not reopened: the thinning is uniform (not oldest-first, the history's rule), the recording runs while the line is shown even with the tool closed, a recording is cleared when the slice is switched off, there is no pause button (switch the slice off to stop), and the model's own cells are not recorded. The existing bundle budget is reused again: entry 195 kB and all JS 1000 kB gzip are unchanged and no check was weakened. The in-thread engine and the demo engine stay in the APK (D10 default). The version in `package.json` and `android/app/build.gradle` stays 0.4.0 (versionCode 7) until the release is cut, so a debug build of this tree still says 0.4.0. Commit trailers carry no model name.


## 20. 0.5.0 as built (2026-10-18)

**Status.** Stage E1 of workstream E (section 9) is built and merged on the designated branch, and was checked by three independent verifiers on the real app after the merge (v-engine the engine, v-render the layer, the frame and the budgets, v-ui the words, the controls and the tool); two fix rounds followed them, and what each did about a finding is written in the same row, in 20.3 and in 20.4. What it holds: the zoom-adaptive wind glyphs ("Arrows and flow lines", 9.2 to 9.4), the Wind height control (9.5, 9.7), the Air slices tool's Map view tab, and an owner requirement that is not in section 9, a "Turn off the slice" button (20.3 item 19). **The owner split E: E1b (colour the map by speed) and E2 (the 60 m fire-felt raster, `fireWindU/V` in the snapshot) are not built, and may become 0.5.1.** No simulation, snapshot, fire, fuel or terrain code changed, and the bundle budget is unchanged (entry 195 kB, all JS 1000 kB gzip). versionCode 9; `package.json` and `android/app/build.gradle` still say 0.4.1 (versionCode 8) until the release is cut. All of it ran on software rendering and in node: **nothing has been seen on a phone**. The gate results are in 20.2. Where 20.3 contradicts section 9, the design file or 18.4, 20.3 wins.
