| `scripts/layout-budget.mjs` | [gate] ok at five sizes: 73.9 / 76.0 / 70.3 / 65.6 / 77.4 % band at 390x844, 412x915, 360x740, 360x640, 844x390 (budgets 72 / 74 / 68 / 63 / 72), the same as 0.4.1 (0.4.1: 73.9 / 76.0 / 70.3 / 65.6 / 77.4 % at 390x844, 412x915, 360x740, 360x640, 844x390; budgets 72 / 74 / 68 / 63 / 72) |
| `scripts/audit-tap-targets.mjs` | [gate] ok on the Setup screen and on the sim screen at 412x915, 844x390 and 360x640 (every visible control has a 44 x 44 px hit area); the real-engine audit of the tool on both tabs, with the off button, runs inside `air-slice.spec.ts`. The gate runs it on `?mock=1`, where there is no slice, so it never reaches the tabs, the Map view's controls or *Turn off the slice*: `e2e/air-tool.spec.ts` and `e2e/air-slice.spec.ts` run the same tap logic on the real tool at 412 x 915 and 360 x 640 (the 844 x 390 limit of 19.4 stands, 20.4) |
| `npx playwright test` (the whole e2e suite) | [gate] 151 of 154 passed on the first run (1.2 h). The three failures were load-sensitive and pass alone on the quiet machine: `airslice-panel.spec.ts:247` (reduced motion, 35.3 s) and `pace.spec.ts:72` (real-engine slow tier at 3600x, 55.4 s), the same two specs that failed the first full runs of 0.4.0 and 0.4.1, and `wind-glyphs.spec.ts:538` (the 2-D stand-in map: it waited 5 s for the safety notice at the start of the page on a loaded run, before any glyph code ran; 2.3 s alone). No defect was found behind any of them. `robustness.spec.ts` (which the fix lanes did not re-run after Escape began to close the top layer from a radio, checkbox or slider) passed in the full run. New or changed specs: `wind-glyphs.spec.ts` (4 tests), `air-tool.spec.ts` (2 new), `air-slice.spec.ts` (Turn off the slice in the recording flow), `layers.spec.ts` (5 new), `robustness.spec.ts` (the switch and every chip), `debug-stats.spec.ts`, `ui.spec.ts` (the four new Help terms), `gestures.spec.ts` and `gestures-edge.spec.ts` (the `Hand` move, no assertion changed), `gallery.spec.ts` (`gallery 0.5.0`, 2 tests). `robustness.spec.ts` was not re-run by fix lane B after Escape began to close the top layer from a radio, a checkbox or a slider in every panel of the sim screen (any spec that pressed Escape on a focused input expecting nothing would now see the layer close); the gate runs it |
| Debug APK | [release] `npm run build:cap`, `npx cap sync android`, `./gradlew assembleDebug`: `app-debug.apk` 30,384,251 B (29.0 MiB), `au.firesim.app`, versionCode 9, versionName 0.5.0 (`output-metadata.json`), debug-signed. The Setup-screen tap audit with the notice accepted passes at 390 and 360 widths. Bundle on the release build: entry 169.2 kB gzip of 195, all JS 963.5 of 1000, CSS 129.1 kB raw (23.5 gzip), demo data 33.1 MB. **Nothing has been seen on a phone** (`npm run build:cap`, `npx cap sync android`, `./gradlew assembleDebug`). **Nothing has been seen on a phone** |

Not run by the docs lane: the whole unit and e2e suites (it ran by path the two test files it changed, `flowGlyphs.perf.test.ts` and `flowGlyphs.alloc.test.ts` for the node figures, `tsc`, the `gallery 0.5.0` tests and the Help test of `ui.spec.ts`, which passed), the contrast and layout audits, the APK, and the phone.

### 20.3 Corrections to the plan and the design (what the builds and verifiers proved)

1. 9.2, 9.3 and 9.7, the key and the motion: `windGlyphs` is a boolean (fourteen sibling switches are booleans), not `'off' | 'on'`. There are no moving dots and no 250 ms cross-fade: dots would have to switch `animating` on (the layer must not), and a cross-fade needs a second pair of batches (four draw calls, and the old and new buffers alive together). A level change swaps the new picture in at once. Reduced motion has nothing to switch off, so there is no branch for it. **(0.6.0 corrects the last two sentences: at level 4 the layer animates, on the GPU, and reduced motion has a branch; 21.3 item 1.)**
2. 9.2, 9.5, 9.7 and D11, the heights: the keys are `'fire' | 50 | 100 | 300 | 1000 | 2000`, with **no 10 m chip next to a "What the fire feels" chip**. The sampler clamps below the model's first level, whose middle is 15 m above the ground (12.5 m on the High tier: `dz1 / 2`), so a numeric 10 m chip would show the 15 m wind beside a chip that shows the 10 m wind: two nearly equal pictures. The first chip is the key `'fire'`, labelled **"10 m"**: it is `surfaceU/V`, the model's wind about 10 m above open ground, the field the Wind speed map shows, and it is not what the fire feels (plan 18.4 measured the calm-night difference on the fast tier: p10 0.34, p90 2.2 of the published wind). The design file called it "Ground"; v-ui found that "Ground" contradicted the caption's "about 10 m above". The caption says it is not the wind the fire feels ("On a calm night the fire can feel much less wind than this in places, and much more in others."). E2 would rename the chip "What the fire feels"; the key stays.
3. 9.8, the triangle budget: 2,000 glyphs x 12 segments is 48k triangles **for one batch**; the halo and the colour stroke are two, so 96k at the cap, and 495k + 96k is over the 550k of a High frame. The budget is **segments** (`GLYPH_CAPS`: 600 / 1,200 / 2,000 glyphs and 4,000 / 8,000 / 12,000 segments for the Low, Medium and High tiers, at most 12 segments a glyph: 10 for the shaft and two for the head), and the layer trims itself from `renderer.info` against `FRAME_TRIANGLE_LIMIT` (550,000). Verified (v-render): the largest High frame with everything on was 546,580 triangles, Medium 380.9k, Low 243.9k, and at a faked 546k, 549k and 552k the trim left room for 1,000, 250 and 0 segments with no flapping. When the rest of the frame is already at the limit the layer trims to 0 segments and says so (`idle: 'budget'`: "Not drawn here: this view already uses all the phone can draw. Zoom out or switch a layer off."), and builds again by itself once a tenth of the tier's cap fits. **(0.6.0: `segmentCap` takes the triangles a segment, 4 for the glyph pair and 2 for the detailed streaks; 21.3 item 3. Corrected again after the detail verifier: the layer trims itself to the limit for the arrows and flow lines only, and the detailed streaks keep a floor over it, so "a frame is never planned past the limit" is no longer true of them; 21.3 item 18.)**
4. 9.3, `flow2d.ts` and the fallback: `flow2d.ts` exports only a slice-plane particle stepper on `LatticeStencil`, and nothing in it traces a plan-view streamline, so it is not shared; the engine is standalone. "Direct sampling is the fallback" is false as a runtime path: tracing straight through the sampler is the **test oracle only** (`glyphOracle.testing.ts`), and the raster fill calls `windP`, never `wind()` or `surfaceWind()` (boxed numbers, 62 bytes a call un-inlined; sampler v2 was built as `windP` with 0 bytes, 18.3 item 1, not the 48 bytes a call of 9.3).
5. 9.2, the legend: `windLegend()` already had a production caller (`mapLegend.ts` through `SceneLegends.wind`), so streaks had a legend card in 0.4.1, and only the dev arrows had none. The glyphs' card is `windGlyphLegend(heightLabel)` through the same path, titled "Wind near the ground" for the 10 m chip or "Wind at 100 m", in km/h; the precedence is heat map, then coded trees, then the slice, then the glyphs, then the streaks, and the card is not shown at eye level (nothing is drawn there).
6. 9.4, arrowheads: `StreakBatch`'s head (`uHeadPx`) is one value for a whole batch, so it cannot draw a polyline; the heads are two chevron segments (+/-28 degrees from the reversed end tangent, 5 to 9 px, 0.35 of the length) in a plain batch with `headPx: 0`, an alpha of 1 and no fog (the legend's colours stay exact). The "+2 draw calls" of 9.4 holds (halo and colour), and removing `WindArrows` saves none (it was hidden). **(0.6.0: the "+2 draw calls" holds for L1 to L3; at L4 the detailed streaks draw one; 21.3 item 2.)**
7. 9.3 and 9.5, where the numbers come from: sampling is on the **10 m ground as received** and drawing on the render mesh (22 to 40 m cells) 1.5 m or 3 px above it, not "above the render DEM" as 9.3 and 9.5 say; plan section 4 rule 10 and the slice (`SceneView.sliceTerrain`) say numbers use the 10 m ground, and the convention `zAsl = ground + h` is the slice presets' and the side view's, so the Map view and the side view agree. The cost of the difference (a verifier's measurement): the mesh and the 10 m ground differ 0.8 m on average (the verifier also reported 14 m and a 95th percentile of 36 m on slopes over 45 degrees); the wind read at the two grounds differs 0.1 % overall and 2 % on average (7 % at the 95th percentile) on steep slopes at 50 to 100 m; the lift buries 0.03 % of the samples.
8. 9.4, the pixel scale: the world step is not `distance x uPixelMetres`. `uPixelMetres` is per **device** pixel (the drawing buffer's height), so a glyph's size would scale with the pixel ratio; the engine uses CSS pixels (`pxPerMetre1 = cssH / (2 tan(fov / 2))`, a seed's metres per pixel = its clip depth / `pxPerMetre1`).
9. 9.3 and 9.4, the visible region, eye level and orbit density: `pickGround` is not used (it is bound to the DOM rectangle and marches a ray; the scout estimated 2.4 ms on a desktop for the eight calls). The visible ground is an analytic intersection of eight view rays with the plane of the lowest terrain, and an exact per-seed projection test culls what the box over-counts. `CameraRig` needed no change (`viewFov` and the canvas height are public). **Eye level draws nothing** (the target is one metre away, so "model cells a pixel" means nothing there), which 9.2 did not say. In an orbit view the density follows the **ground area a pixel covers** (a seed is kept with probability 1 / s2, s2 clamped to 64), not the distance, so it does not rise toward the horizon; over a top view of flat ground s2 is 1 everywhere and a pan moves no seed in or out.
10. 9.7, the Layers panel's DOM: "+4 to 6 nodes against 86 of headroom" was wrong twice. The row is 15 nodes while the switch is off and 51 once it is on (the chips, the caption and the zoom line are built the first time the switch is on, like the air slice's box, and hidden, not torn down, when it goes off again), and the headroom was not 86: the open panel was 940 nodes at 0.3.0 and was not measured again at 0.4.0. The e2e (`layers.spec.ts`) logged: 940 nodes without the row, 955 with it off, 991 with it on (asserted at most 1,000), and **1,118** with the air slice's box, a heat map's legend and the coded trees' legend also shown (over 1,000 without the new row at all). So the headroom is 9 nodes, and **any further Layers row must build lazily**, like the chips.
11. 9.9, the `Hand` helper: the CDP multi-touch helper was in **three** spec files, not two (`gestures.spec.ts`, `gestures-edge.spec.ts` and a slimmer one in `air-slice.spec.ts`). The first two now import the fuller copy from `e2e/helpers.ts` (`Hand`, `newHand`, `frame`; no assertion changed); the slimmer third is left as it is.
12. 9.2, the ladder as built: the lattice spacings of the curved levels are **minimums**. The lattice is the next power of two of metres that is not denser than the level's spacing (L3 36 to 72 px, L4 56 to 112 px on the screen), and for the straight levels the nearest (0.7 to 1.4 times the spacing), with 0.06 octave of give past an edge, so one zoom gives one lattice from either direction (F1). The L3 and L4 glyph counts are about half of the first build's. The levels themselves (edges 4, 12, 32 and 90 px a model cell, 15 % hysteresis) are as planned. A glyph's length is 4 px per m/s clamped to 8 and 45 px (L1), 8 and 32 (L2), 28 and 56 (L3) and, at 6 px per m/s, 90 and 140 (L4), constant in screen pixels, so it saturates at 11.25, 8, 14 and 23.3 m/s and the colour carries the speed above that; the shaft of a curved glyph is 6 to 10 equal steps (`ceil(length / 7 px)`). **(0.6.0: the levels, edges and hysteresis stand; the numbers of L4 (the lattice, the jitter and the length of 6 px per m/s clamped to 90 and 140) are now those of the detailed streaks unless they are not drawn; 21.3 item 4. The detailed streaks' lattice is not a power of two of metres at all: it is nested and about 32 px apart at every zoom; 21.3 item 17.)**
13. Two rules that are not in section 9. **The cliff rule** (F3): a glyph is draped on the drawn ground, so a flow line that met an escarpment ran down its face as a long upright streak that reads as an updraft; no step of a glyph may now climb or fall more than 1 m per metre along the ground (`GLYPH_MAX_SLOPE = 1`, 45 degrees of true slope, whatever the vertical exaggeration): a glyph is cut at the foot or the lip of a wall with its head there, one that starts on a wall is not drawn, and a barb over a wall drops its glyph. Before it 3.4 to 6.8 % of the segments (8 to 21 % of the glyphs) at three Katoomba escarpment views were upright; after it 0.0 % in 12 cliff views (in node 8 % of the glyphs are cut or dropped over 16 L3 pictures: Katoomba is cliff-heavy). **The depth bias** (F5): the strokes lie 1.5 m above the ground under the drawn canopy, so at 700 m and 55 degrees about half of every flow line was behind a crown. The streak shader takes `uDepthBias` (metres toward the eye along each end's own ray, for the depth test only, so the stroke stays where it is on the screen; 0 for every other user of `StreakBatch`), and the layer asks for `GLYPH_DEPTH_BIAS_M = 40`: a crown of up to about 25 m in front of a stroke no longer hides it, a ridge still does. Switching the depth test off was rejected because every line then shows behind every ridge. Halo-ink pixels at 700 m and 55 degrees: 6,645 without, 16,243 with (across a valley at 65 degrees 3,304 against 3,693).
14. 11, the sizes: the engine is 5.9 kB gzip (not about 7), the layer and the scheduler about 3.1 kB (the lane's `rolldown --minify` and `gzip -9` of the shipped modules, not repeated by the docs lane); they are in the render chunk, which loads when the simulation screen builds the view. The entry holds only `glyphTypes.ts` (types and constants), `glyphWords.ts` and `windGlyphControls.ts`, and two tests (`glyphDeps.test.ts` for `src/ui` and the layer catalog, `windGlyphLayer.test.ts` for those, `layers.ts` and `legends.ts`) fail if anything there imports the engine, the scheduler or the layer. The whole feature, with the controls, the Map view tab and Turn off the slice: **+14.2 kB gzip of all JS** (the entry +3.2: the words, the controls, the tab, the off button, the catalog entry and the prefs; the render chunk +8.8: the engine, the layer, the scheduler and the shader's bias, net of the deleted `WindArrows`, close to the lane's 5.9 kB for the engine plus 3.1 kB for the layer and the scheduler; the other +2.2 is spread over the remaining chunks and was not broken down further: this release's Help and Glossary entries are in the `content` chunk) (the plan's range for E was 10 to 14 kB, lazy: the total is at the top of it, and the entry's +3.2 is the part that cannot be lazy, because the Layers panel and the tool's shell are entry modules; the scout had estimated +4 to +6 for the entry and +10 to +15 in all).
15. 9.8 and 11, the rebuild budget: the plan's "8 ms per slice" holds at a 4x CPU throttle for the **whole** set of slices once fix V2 clocked the raster fill: the slice 95th percentile was 8.1 to 8.5 ms (the maximum up to 13 ms on a loaded machine), where the numeric height's raster fill alone had been one slice of p95 14.0 ms. A rebuild is at most four a second (250 ms from the last swap; the first picture and a settled frame are never held), in slices of at most 8 ms (a slice stops on the clock, which is read every 4 rows of the raster fill, every 8 glyphs of the trace and every 256 lattice cells of the walk for the first four rebuilds of a builder, every 32 and 2,048 after them, so a slice overshoots by at most that), and a rebuild finishes in 1 to 3 slices. The first switch-on makes no work by itself: the first picture arrives about two frames later with a synchronous cost of 0.1 to 1.2 ms at 4x (it was 12 to 21 ms with the warm-up of the design, which was measured not to help and dropped). Engine cost in node: the lanes' and verifiers' runs on a loaded machine gave a High-cap rebuild a median of 1.2 to 1.45 ms, a slice p95 of 1.2 to 2.9 ms, a cold rebuild 25 to 65 ms over about 9 to 10 slices, 93 to 413 bytes allocated a rebuild and +28 kB of heap over 3,000 rebuilds; a docs-lane run of `flowGlyphs.perf.test.ts` and `flowGlyphs.alloc.test.ts` gave 1.52 ms (p95 2.11) for 1,864 glyphs and 11,082 segments, a slice p95 of 1.70 ms at 3.4 ms slices, 93 to 413 bytes and +27.4 kB. Idle: no frame in 3 s with the glyphs on (the layer never switches `animating` on) and an idle tick of 0.04 bytes.
16. 9.8, the draw-call limit of 25 was already exceeded by the 0.4.1 layers with everything on at all three tiers (a baseline of 16 in the top view up to 29: trees 7, the curtain 3, embers 2, roads 2, and homes, zones, names, streaks, flames and smoke one each); the glyph layer adds **exactly two** (18 to 31). The builder's e2e passes because it runs the default layers (16 to 18). The limit is stale, not the layer (20.5). **(0.6.0: at L4 the layer adds one call, not two; 21.3 item 2.)**
17. 9.1, 9.4 and D9, what `WindArrows` was: it was used only by `SceneView` and the render dev page (`dev.ts`, never built into the app), no test or e2e used it, and `windLayer.ts`'s `WindLayer` (the streaks, with `animating`) is untouched. `WindArrows`, the `windArrows` option and `setWindArrows` are deleted; the dev page's `?arrows=1` now switches the new layer on and `&wh=fire|50|100|300|1000|2000` picks its height (anything else stops the page with a message). The glyphs are opt-in and the streaks stay as they were: off at first for the glyphs, on at first for the streaks, two separate controls that can both be on (the pictures may then be cluttered; nothing changes by itself). **(0.6.0: "the pictures may then be cluttered" no longer holds at level 4, where both switches draw one picture; 21.3 item 5.)**
18. The glyphs' colours: the design file, the code comment of `buildSpeedTable` and the notes the docs lane was given say the glyphs wear "the Wind speed map's ramp". They do not. They wear `WIND_RAMP` (pale grey-blue at 0 m/s, near white at 4, pale cyan at 8, blue at 12 and 17, violet at 25 m/s: 0 to 90 km/h on the card), which is the animated Wind streaks' ramp (near the ground) and the Air slice's Speed chip's; the Wind speed map is `WIND_SPEED_RAMP`, a cool-to-warm ramp from 0 to 60 km/h and over. What the 10 m chip shares with the Wind speed map is the **field** (`surfaceU/V`), not the colours. `truthAudit.test.ts` holds that no document says otherwise.
19. Not in section 9, an owner requirement: **Turn off the slice**. A plain secondary button (`slice-off`, `btn btn-secondary btn-lg`, the row pattern of the other buttons, no new CSS: `slice.css` stays 4,066 bytes under a guard of 4,096) is the last row of the side view's controls and of the Map view. One press (`turnOffSlice`, `ToolStep`) writes `enabled: false` and clears the stored line (`a` and `b` undefined, `preset: null`; the chip, `azimuth` and `centre` stay) and the tool's placement, candidate, draft end and probe, and the one layers write clears the curtain, the panel's slice, the marks and the probe's pin on the map, the recording, and the Layers panel reads Off. The stored line is cleared on purpose: a kept line would make the tool's next opening switch the slice back on (`open()` does that for a line the Layers panel only switched off) and look as if the button had failed. The tool **stays open** on the empty placement state; focus goes to *Set start (A)* on the side view and to the switch on the Map view; the polite announcement is "Slice turned off." or, when a recording was held, "Slice turned off. The recording was cleared."; a hint under the button, "This also clears the recording.", shows only while the recorder holds a picture and the button points at it (`aria-describedby`). There is no pop-up, no confirmation and no undo. The button is **absent** (hidden, not disabled) when there is nothing to turn off, and also while a placement is under way and **for a lone draft end** (v-ui: the row made the sheet 72 px taller after "Place A here" and moved the cross). Closing the tool (X, Back, Escape) still does not turn the slice off (19.5), and the Layers panel's own switch-off still keeps the line, so the tool brings it back.
20. 4.1 of the design and the plan's Map view tab, as built: the two views are a **tablist** of tab buttons in the segmented look (`role="tablist"`, `aria-selected`, only the chosen tab in the tab order, Left, Right, Home and End, a tabpanel named by the chosen tab, a polite announcement of "Map view: wind arrows and flow lines." or "Side view."), not the radio `segmented` of the design, and they sit in the panel's header where the subtitle "Side view" was (a body row would have cost 48 px; the header is no taller, at most 50 px at three sizes). Under 400 px the title gives up its icon so that the two words fit at 360 px; the labels stay "Side view" and "Map view". The Map view holds the Layers panel's own switch (named "Wind arrows and flow lines" there, with the one line "Draws the wind on the map: arrows from far away, flow lines when you zoom in." while it is off), the chips, the caption and the zoom line, **one state** with Layers (the same builder, `windGlyphControls.ts`); the side view's panel is taken out of the document and told it is not active (its streaks stop) while the Map view shows, and gets its data again on the way back; a tab press drops a placement under way; the choice of tab is kept when the tool is closed and opened again; on the 2-D map the tool is one line and has no tabs.
21. 9.8, the warm-up: the design said to run one throw-away 48-glyph rebuild when the layer is first enabled ("the first rebuild costs 4 to 7 times a steady one", spike S3). It was built, then measured at a 4x throttle in the real app (three runs each) and dropped (V3): 12 to 21 ms in one frame and then 33 to 40 ms of slices for the first picture, against 29 to 62 ms of slices and no synchronous cost without it.
22. Not the glyphs': 19.3 item 16 said the version stays 0.4.0 until the release is cut; 0.4.1 was cut (`6bd14f6`: `package.json` and `android/app/build.gradle` say 0.4.1, versionCode 8). 0.5.0's own bump (versionCode 9) is the orchestrator's: until it is cut they still say 0.4.1.

### 20.4 Known limitations and what is left

- **Nothing about phones is measured.** The ladder's edges (4, 12, 32 and 90 px a model cell) and how they feel while pinching, the legibility of the halo and the pale stops in sunlight, whether 2.5 px strokes read at a pixel ratio of 3, rebuild hitching while panning with one finger, the battery with the glyphs on during playback, TalkBack on the tabs, the chips and *Turn off the slice*, and the 1.5 m lift on cliffs were judged only in software rendering and in node. Phone check-list 3 (section 12) is the way to find out.
- **Orbit view near the horizon (F2, not fixed):** at a tilt of 68 to 82 degrees the L3 rows stack about 10 to 13 px apart and convergent flow makes bundles. Thinning by the nominal pixel area was not done: it would also thin the default tilt-55 view by about 0.57 at the centre and cannot be tuned without a phone. L4 in orbit looks good.
- Glyph length saturates, so a stronger wind than 8 m/s (L2), 11 m/s (L1) or 14 m/s (L3) is told by colour and not by length. L1 and L2 arrows are one straight chord, so over relief 6 to 17 % of them are partly under the ground (2 to 5 % of their length; 11 % of L1 are buried by more than 10 m, and a cap over a gorge can look like a 7 px hover). Pan invariance is bit-exact only for an overhead camera over flat ground: over relief a glyph's length in metres changes by 2 to 15 % with the camera, and is constant in pixels. The far zoom shows few arrows (26 to 30 at 12 to 18 km, 51 at 14 km, 131 to 268 at 9 to 6 km: the 6 km area is a small part of the screen).
- The legend card is still shown at level 0 (reachable: a model cell of 2.91 px at 844 x 390 at the farthest zoom of a 6 km area), where nothing is drawn, and the zoom line says "Zoom in to see the wind arrows."
- On the fast tier the *Use the 3-D atmosphere* button was never in view unscrolled in 0.4.1 either (behind the sticky footer at 412 x 915, below the sheet at 360 x 640 and 844 x 390) and is now one row (58 px) lower, because *Turn off the slice* is above it. Moving it above the controls is a question for the owner (20.5). At 844 x 390 with a recording the footer wraps to two lines and the real-engine scroll-to-centre tap audit misses 3 of 8 bottom probes of every control below the picture, *Turn off the slice* included (the 0.4.1 limit of 19.4, which was not run on 0.4.1 with a recording). The gate's mock audit passes only because the mock has no slice.
- The e2e of the glyphs ran on the fast tier; the 3-D tier is covered by the air-slice and layers tests and by v-render's six-height check. In v-render's script the live switch from the standard to the fast tier (`setQuality('standard')` on a Fast run) gave no 3-D snapshot in 300 s; that is not glyph code and was not looked at further.
- `flowGlyphs.perf.test.ts` "a warm rebuild of the High cap" is on the wall clock and failed once under load (it passes alone); its cold-start test was moved to a fake clock (V4).
- Escape from a focused radio, checkbox or slider now closes the top layer in every panel of the sim screen, not only the Air slices tool; a spec that pressed Escape on a focused input expecting nothing would see the layer close. `robustness.spec.ts` was not re-run after that change (20.2).
- The Layers panel's DOM headroom is 9 nodes (20.3 item 10): the next row there must build lazily. A user who never switches the layer on pays 1.45 MiB of typed arrays (the builder's output and candidate store, 560,196 bytes, and the two batches' own copies, 960,000 bytes, at the High tier: derived from the allocations in the code, which equals v-render's measurement of 1.45 MiB) and an idle tick of 4.4 microseconds; the raster for a numeric height (226,576 bytes) is made the first time a height is used. Making the layer lazy would save under 2 % of the ArrayBuffer memory and was not done.
- 0.5.1 (E1b, E2) and 0.3.1 (H-sim and the speed test button, waiting for the S6 re-label) are not built.

### 20.5 Decisions taken for you in 0.5.0 (change any of them)

**E was split:** only E1 is in 0.5.0 (your decision); E1b (colour the map by speed) and E2 (the 60 m fire-felt raster) are not built and may become 0.5.1. The layer is **opt-in** (D9) and the animated Wind streaks **stay on at first**, so the two can clutter and nothing changes by itself. **Moving dots and the 250 ms cross-fade were dropped** (they cost `animating` and two more draw calls). The **first chip is "10 m"** (key `'fire'`, the model's open-ground wind, with the calm-night sentence) and **there is no "10 m and fire" pair of chips**; "What the fire feels" waits for E2. **Closing the Air slices tool does not turn the slice off, and the new button does**: *Turn off the slice* leaves the tool open on its empty state, is hidden for a lone draft end, clears the recording without asking (the hint under it is the whole safeguard) and does not come back when the tool is reopened. The arrows' **legend card follows the switch** (hidden at eye level, shown at level 0). **F2 was not fixed** (it cannot be tuned without a phone). **The fast tier's *Use the 3-D atmosphere* button was left where it is** (an open question: above the controls would put it in view). **The layer is not built lazily** (it would save under 2 % of the ArrayBuffer memory). **The plan's draw-call limit of 25 is stale** (16 to 29 with everything on before the layer and 18 to 31 with it; information, the layer adds two). The existing bundle budget is reused again: entry 195 kB and all JS 1000 kB gzip are unchanged and no check was weakened. The in-thread engine and the demo engine stay in the APK (D10 default). The version in `package.json` and `android/app/build.gradle` stays 0.4.1 (versionCode 8) until the release is cut, so a debug build of this tree still says 0.4.1. Commit trailers carry no model name.


---

## 21. 0.6.0 as built (2026-10-30)

**Status.** Five requests of the owner are built and merged on the designated branch: (1) the **Wind detail chip**, (2) the **detailed streaks** at the most detailed zoom level (L4), (3) the **two-finger turn inverted** (touch only), (4) **Wind direction**, a tool that turns the wind of the whole map from a time you choose, in place of the old per-place Local wind tool, and (5) **twice as many streaks and arrows** in the Air slice's side view and on its 3-D curtain (21 columns by nine heights instead of 15 by six; the verifier of the side view found the curtain's arrows had been left out, and the orchestrator doubled them, `4a9573e`). They are in the owner's words in the table below. Independent verifiers were checking them on the real app and on the real engine while this section was written, and the fixes they had made by then are in the rows of 21.1 (their own verdict lines were not among the docs lane's inputs, so the verdict cells are inferred from the fixes they merged, as in 20.1, and the orchestrator's record is in 21.x). **Two fix lanes merged after the first write-up, and 21.1 (the last five rows) and 21.3 items 17 to 20 record them: the detailed streaks' lattice now nests and is about 32 px apart at every zoom, and the detailed streaks are never trimmed to the frame, so they go over the 550,000 triangle limit by at most 24,000.** **Two of the five touch the simulation or its inputs: item 4 changes the simulation by design (a Wind direction change is a scenario edit) and is deterministic, with no change every result is hash-identical to 0.5.0 on both tiers (the independent verifier's check); items 1, 2, 3 and 5 are display only** and nothing in them reaches a snapshot, the history or the engine. The bundle budget is unchanged (entry 195 kB, all JS 1000 kB gzip; no check was weakened). versionCode 10; the version files are bumped by the release. E1b (colour the map by speed) and E2 (the 60 m fire-felt raster) are **not** built (0.5.1 was never authorised) and 0.3.1 (rock in the fire model) still waits for the S6 re-label. All of it ran on software rendering and in node: **nothing has been seen on a phone**. Where 21.3 contradicts sections 9, 20.3 or the design files of 0.6.0 (`docs/plans/0.6.0/`), 21.3 wins.

| "The wind should change visualisation when it gets to its most detailed level; the wind streaks should become much more detailed and believable." | **Detailed streaks** at level 4: comet-shaped, speed-coloured, with small eddies added for looks, one picture for both switches, one draw call | D-engine, D-render, integration |
| (the two-finger turn goes the wrong way round) | a drag to the right turns the camera anticlockwise seen from above; touch only | G |
| "The Local wind feature should be a global wind direction changing feature called just simply Wind direction. You give it a new wind direction and an optional time and it changes the wind direction then." | **Wind direction**: the whole map's wind turns from the chosen time (default now) over 15 minutes and holds | W-core, W-ui, W-fix |

### 21.3 Corrections to the plan and the design (what the builds and verifiers proved)

1. 20.3 item 1 and the header of `windGlyphLayer.ts` (and 9.4: "the glyphs never animate"): **at level 4 the layer animates**, on the GPU. `animating` is true only while the detailed streaks are drawn in motion; L1 to L3 still never animate, and with the Wind streaks on (the default) the old CPU particle loop is hidden while the detailed streaks are drawn, so the net CPU work per drawn frame falls (the scout measured the old loop at a median of 0.55 ms on a desktop). With the arrows alone, level 4 starts animating where 0.5.0 sat still: the one deliberate change to item 1. 20.3 item 1's "Reduced motion has nothing to switch off, so there is no branch for it" is no longer true: `SceneView` reads `matchMedia('(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)')` and its change event and tells the layer (`setMotion`), and `stats().detail` is `'still'`: the batch draws one frozen frame (3.7 s of the shader's clock, where most lines carry a pulse) and nothing animates. The old Wind particles still ignore reduced motion (existing, not changed).
2. 20.3 items 6 and 16, and 9.4 and 9.8 ("+2 draw calls"): the **+2** holds for L1 to L3 only. At level 4 the glyph batches are empty and the layer draws **one** call (the detail batch, 2 triangles a segment, hence at most 24,000 triangles at the High cap against 96,000 for the glyph pair). The integration lane measured at 412 x 915 on the real engine: level 3 adds 3 draw calls and 8,392 triangles, level 4 adds 1 and 1,704 (852 segments, 76 lines; **corrected: those 852 segments and 76 lines were a picture trimmed by the frame in dense forest before the floor of item 18 (inferred, not re-measured); at 412 x 915 the closest zoom now holds about 460 to 860 lines**). The docs lane did not repeat these and could not reconcile "level 3 adds 3" with 0.5.0's "the glyph layer adds exactly two" (20.3 item 16), and says so.
3. 20.3 item 3 (the triangle budget) and 9.8: `segmentCap(tierSegments, framesTriangles, ownSegments, trianglesPerSegment = 4)` takes the triangles a segment: 4 for the glyph pair and 2 for the detail batch (`DETAIL_TRIANGLES`); the caps stay 4,000 / 8,000 / 12,000 segments by picture tier, `FRAME_TRIANGLE_LIMIT` 550,000 stays, and `idle: 'budget'` still says it when nothing fits. **(Corrected: `segmentCap` has a fifth argument, `floorSegments`, which the detailed streaks use to stay over the limit, and `budget` is now only ever said for the arrows and flow lines; item 18.)**
4. 20.3 item 12, 9.2 and the README's ladder table: **L4 is no longer "long flow lines" when the detailed streaks are drawn.** The levels, their edges (4, 12, 32 and 90 px a model cell) and the 15 % hysteresis are as planned. With `planLod(..., detail = true)` the last level takes the numbers of `DETAIL_GEOMETRY`: a lattice of 40 px (the 0.5.0 rule of the next power of two of metres that is not denser, so 40 to 80 px on the screen; **corrected twice: 32 px (59b4d9e), then not one lattice per octave at all but a nested one, about 32 px apart on average at every zoom, item 17**), jitter 0.35 (0.3), a line of 16 px per m/s clamped to 90 to 220 px (6 px per m/s, 90 to 140), traced in steps of 9 px by RK2 in 8 to 24 segments (6 to 10), always curved. Without the flag every 0.5.0 number and test stands. The long flow lines are still drawn at the closest zoom when the detailed streaks are not (the detail not built yet; **the layer is no longer trimmed to nothing there, item 18**), and the zoom line says "Showing long flow lines." then, "Showing streaks with small eddies, standing still. This is the most detailed level." under reduced motion, and "Showing moving streaks with small eddies. This is the most detailed level." otherwise.
5. 20.3 item 17 (the glyphs are opt-in, the streaks on at first, "the pictures may then be cluttered"): at level 4 **both switches draw one and the same picture**, so there is no clutter there, and the old particles step aside only while it is drawn (`WindLayer.setSuppressed`, `detailShown`), giving themselves back when a picture is trimmed to nothing. The through-the-plume streaks (`wind: 'volume'`) are untouched. Colour is `WIND_RAMP` as for the arrows (20.3 item 18 holds).
6. 20.3 item 14 and 11 (the budgets): the engine (`detailStreaks.ts`) and the batch (`detailBatch.ts`) are in the lazy render chunk; the entry holds the chip's state, pips and chip (`windDetailState.ts`, `windDetailPips.ts`, `windDetailChip.ts`), the Wind direction panel and `windDirection.ts`; the chip's sheet and its words are **one lazy chunk of about 1.27 kB gzip**. The integration lane measured the entry at about 172 kB (0.5.0: 169.2) and all JS at about 979.8 kB (963.5) for the whole release (after the detailed streaks' fix lanes: 172.7 and 980.5); no budget was raised. The scout's lane budget for the wind detail (entry +2.5 kB, all JS +8 kB) was for the two wind detail requests alone.
7. 9.2, the chip's reasons (the notes the lanes were given said "tier, model cell size, drawing budget, eye level, Wind height, both wind layers off"): as built, the sheet's **limits** are *the zoom*, *the model's cells*, *the drawing budget*, *the tier* (only the fast tier's diagnosed wind, "estimated ... not simulated") and *reduced motion*; **eye level and both layers off do not appear in the sheet because they hide the chip** (eye level with only the arrows on; at eye level with the Wind streaks on the chip stays and shows their one pip), and the Wind height is a line of the sheet ("Wind about 10 m above open ground." or "Wind at about 100 m above the ground."), not a limit. The chip is not shown for the Wind speed map (a heat map), in the 2-D map, or while a panel covers the map.
8. 8.5 (the panel's streaks "about 300 particles", line 432 above) and ARCHITECTURE and README (`about 300 particles`, `0.05 ms and 2.6 bytes`): the side view has **about 600** particles (one per 95 square px, minimum 120; 190 before) and a still arrow lattice of 24 x 21 px a cell (34 x 30), the longest arrow 60 % of the smaller side of its cell (about 7 to 13 px on a phone; 0.5.0 drew 10 to 18 px). One step of 600 particles takes 0.085 ms in a docs-lane run (0.05 ms for the 300), with the same 2.6 bytes. The code comment in `render/slice/types.ts` was the same stale 300. The 3-D curtain's wind arrows are 21 columns by nine heights (30 to 2,200 m above the ground; 168 on a flat line against 75 for the 15 by six of 8.6): the side view's verifier found them missed and the orchestrator doubled them (`4a9573e`). What the verifier measured on the real app is in 21.1.
9. The two-finger turn depends on **two private members of three's `OrbitControls` (r186)**, `_rotateLeft` and `_handleTouchMoveDollyRotate`, which its type declarations leave out (`TwoFingerTurnControls` reaches them through `OrbitControls.prototype` and a small interface). A three upgrade must re-check the sign tests of `cameraRig.gestures.test.ts` (54 tests) and `e2e/gestures.spec.ts`. The README said eye level lets "one or two fingers turn the view"; it is one finger, a second pauses it (the 0.6.0 comment of lane G), and the README is corrected.
10. The tool is "Wind direction". The old tool's panel was titled "Local wind" in the app and "Wind" in the Tools list, and it **placed** a reading at a point with a speed and a radius; the documents only ever called it "Wind", so the docs lane replaced the places that described it: Help's "Fuel / Wind" step ("the wind here") and Tools list, the README's Controls row (the other tools "(fire, fuel, wind) still place on the ground"), and DESIGN's remark that the Air slices tool's placement is "the two-step confirm the Wind tool already uses" (it is the Fire and Fuel tools'). The user-facing name is "Wind direction" everywhere; "local wind reading" survives only for the belt kit's `WindEdit`. `WindEdit` stays in the scenario for Setup's belt-kit readings (online only); the panel lists them under "Wind readings" with a Remove for each (W-fix), where the scout's design had accepted that they could no longer be removed in a run (design 1 item 8, reversed).
11. The scout's design said the forecast's own wind-change cards, timeline marks, Danger gate and Weather callout would "still describe the forecast" (accepted). W-fix reversed it: once a change has started, the forecast's later swings are replaced and **are not shown as coming** (the Weather tab says one line, "You changed the wind direction, so the forecast's later wind changes no longer apply."; the chart, the timeline and the Insights list do not mark or list them; the engine raises no wind-change or dead-man-zone card and no Danger zone for them); a swing before the first change starts is still shown; removing the change brings it all back. **A known gap** is in 21.4.
12. The scout's design (`D-CONTRACT-060-wind.md`, 1 item 1) put the 3-D lag at "the lowest layer within a minute, layers above 45 min"; its own measurement said 5 to 10 minutes and a 10 to 20 degree overshoot near the ground (9 km test), and the contract's estimate for the fire's wind was 17 to 25 degrees. What the independent verifier measured on the real engine (Katoomba 6 km): on the **fast tier** the fire's wind follows the ambient turn within about **6 degrees**; on the **standard tier (3-D air)** the ground wind can lag or overshoot the ambient by up to about **15 degrees** during the turn and settles within minutes. **The contract's 17 to 25 degrees was not reproduced**; `windDirection.sim.test.ts` still allows 25 degrees while it turns and 5 degrees 30 minutes after. The Help says the wind on the 3-D settings can lag a little during and after a big turn.
13. What the independent verifier also measured (Katoomba 6 km, the real engine): with **no change the results are identical to before the feature** (hash-identical on both tiers); the same change twice is deterministic; the cost is about **+2.2 s over a 60-minute standard-tier run in the worker** (+0.4 s on the fast tier), with no stall (the build machine's own runs gave +2.2 to 2.6 s); the cost is one more background solve for each stamp the turn made when it is crossed. The docs lane did not repeat any of these.
14. The engine found two things the contract's text did not say: `stepOnce` captured the weather series **before** `applyDueRecords`, so the stamp pair must be read after the records are applied; and `Simulation.removeEdit` records "from now on", so "as if never added" needed its own branch, which is `removeIgnition`'s body extracted as `undoRecords(match)` and shared. A change given with the scenario (`scenario.edits`) keeps its own time in the session's list (which listed every scenario edit at time 0) and, at time 0, is applied by the first step like an ignition.
15. The settled decision that a change's time is **a whole minute** (the display step must not move it: rule 1 of section 4) means "Now" starts up to 59 s after the view time and the status shows that clock minute; the last minute of a scenario takes no change; "At a time" is the first such clock time at or after the "Now" start in the scenario's own zone, never the device's.
16. Not the 0.6.0 lanes': 20.3 item 22 said 0.5.0's own version bump (versionCode 9) was the orchestrator's; it was cut (`5da3c92`, `package.json` and `android/app/build.gradle` say 0.5.0). 0.6.0's bump (versionCode 10) is the orchestrator's: until it is cut they still say 0.5.0.
17. 9.2, 20.3 item 12 and items 4 and 6 above, the detailed streaks' density (the detail verifier and the D-fix lane, `e823616`): the lattice is **nested and dyadic**, not one power of two of metres per octave of zoom. Why: a lattice of one power of two of metres, 32 to 64 px apart, lost three quarters of its lines through each octave of zoom and got them back, with the whole picture replaced, at the octave's edge (measured on the real app at 412 x 915 over 58 zoom steps of 6 %: the worst ratio between neighbouring steps was 3.76). How it is now: the points of a finest lattice of 2^j metres and of every coarser one (twice, four times, ... the spacing), each a subset of the finer; **a point's identity is the coarsest lattice that holds it, and everything about it (its place, whether it is thinned, its cap hash, its life and its orbit hash) is a hash of that identity**, so it is the same point at every zoom and a line keeps its animation phase. `planLod` gives the finest octave (`spacingExp`) and `thin`, a share from 1/4 to 1 that follows the zoom continuously (additive: `LodPlan.thin?`, `BuiltPicture.thin?`, `PictureNow.thin?`, no new contract type). The finer lattice at `thin` 1/4 equals the coarser at 1, line for line (unit-tested bytewise), so the lines are about 32 px apart on average at **every** zoom (`DETAIL_GEOMETRY.spacingPx` 32 now means that: it was 40, then 32 px at the octave's fine end), there is no pop and no fourfold density swing at an octave edge (worst neighbour ratio 1.08, against 3.76 before), and about 460 to 860 lines are drawn at 412 x 915. A new rebuild reason, `density` (`THIN_REBUILD_RATIO` 1.1, about a 5 % zoom), keeps the picture following the zoom under the existing 250 ms throttle; the 10 % pose-zoom rule is kept. The README, ARCHITECTURE and DESIGN say the same.
18. 9.8, 20.3 item 3 and items 2 and 3 above, the triangle budget (the detail verifier and the D-fix lane, `53ea9e3`, `5dc7f1b`): **the detailed streaks are never trimmed to the frame, so the layer no longer keeps the frame inside 550,000 triangles at level 4.** `segmentCap(tier, frame, own, trianglesPerSegment = 4, floorSegments = 0)` takes a floor, `DETAIL_MIN_SEGMENTS` (the High tier's cap, 12,000 segments, so the whole cap of every tier), used for the detailed streaks only; the glyph pair (levels 1 to 3) still trims and says `budget`. The High tier's worst case is the limit plus 2 x 12,000 = 24,000 triangles (4.4 %), and only the streaks go over it; the measured worst frame was 594,742 triangles (Katoomba forest, closest zoom, High), of which the other layers alone were already 576,104 (terrain 318,000, canopy about 200,000, roads 21,000). Why: before, in dense forest at the High tier the cap went to 0 from 454 m down to the closest zoom (43 of the 58 steps of level 4 were empty), and the owner's request needs a picture at the closest zoom; a first floor of 8,000 segments was then raised to the whole cap so that the same view gives the same picture however full the frame was when it was built. The chip's and the sheet's words are unchanged: the budget line is now only ever shown for the arrows and flow lines. The e2e specs state the new worst case (`FRAME_TRIANGLE_LIMIT + 2 * DETAIL_MIN_SEGMENTS`).
19. 21.2, the cost of the detailed streaks after the nested lattice (the D-fix lane and the detail verifier; the docs lane did not repeat them): a node rebuild of a High build a median of about 2.5 ms (1.9 ms before: four times more cells are walked); a phone-size orbit view (570 to 720 lines) 1.4 to 1.8 ms; the worst case, a 2560 x 1440 orbit view, 19 ms in 6 slices; the heap flat over 3,000 rebuilds, 330 to 540 bytes allocated a rebuild. In the browser (software GL, a shared machine) the whole rebuild is 8 to 9.5 ms on average in 2 slices, and at a 4x CPU slowdown the slice p95 is 8.5 ms and the maximum 11.1 ms. The **cold first zoom into level 4** at 4x costs slices of 10 to 15 ms against the 8 ms budget and 2 or 3 long tasks of 52 to 98 ms, once per session (21.4). Bundle: the entry 172.7 kB gzip (budget 195) and all JavaScript 980.5 kB (budget 1000).
20. Test infrastructure and an environment note. `e2e/fixtures.ts` now ignores the console warning "No available adapters." that a Chromium with WebGPU but no adapter logs when the debug statistics screen asks for one (`d1d161a`; the older "Failed to create WebGPU Context Provider" was already ignored): it is not the app's and a phone does not log it to a test. And `e2e/air-tool.spec.ts:439` ("Side view and Map view tabs") **fails in this environment on the 0.5.0 tree as well** (its old-rules check sees no broken word; it looks environmental, the fonts), so it is not a regression of 0.6.0.

### 21.4 Known limitations and what is left

- **Nothing about phones is measured.** Phone check-lists 1 to 3 (section 12) are still outstanding, and section 12 now has check-list 4 for 0.6.0. The detailed streaks' real **look and cost** were seen only under software GL: the stroke width (`DETAIL_WIDTH_PX` 3.2), the eddy amplitude, the density (`DETAIL_GEOMETRY`: about 32 px apart at every zoom) and the speed the pulses move at are first guesses; their battery use and the animation of level 4 with the arrows alone (new in 0.6.0) are unmeasured, and so is the chip's place on a small screen against thumbs and gloves. Constants live in `DETAIL_GEOMETRY`, `DETAIL_WIDTH_PX` and one block of the shader.
- **The detailed streaks, after the verifier:** (a) the **cold first zoom into level 4** at a 4x CPU slowdown is slices of 10 to 15 ms against the 8 ms budget with 2 or 3 long tasks of 52 to 98 ms, once per session; (b) the streaks **pass over the crowns of the trees and do not flow around them**; (c) the layer **exceeds the frame's triangle limit by design**, by at most 24,000 triangles (4.4 %) at the High tier, and in dense forest the frame is over the limit without it (21.3 item 18); (d) a few code comments still carry the numbers of the first build (`detailStreaks.perf.test.ts`'s header: about 840 lines of 14 segments; the header of `DETAIL_GEOMETRY`: about 400 to 630 lines over 412 x 915, where the real app measured 460 to 860).
- **The side view's calm arrows are short:** the density rule keeps 40 % of a cell's smaller side clear, so a calm arrow is 7.8 to 8.7 px on a portrait phone and 6.5 to 6.9 px on a landscape one (about 17 device px at a pixel ratio of 2.625), 29 to 42 % shorter than in 0.5.0. A floor of about 8 px is a follow-up and is not built.
- **An environmental failure:** `e2e/air-tool.spec.ts:439` fails in this environment on the 0.5.0 tree as well (21.3 item 20); it is not a regression.
- **The eddies are not the model's.** The sheet says so, and the colour is never changed, but a person could still read a wiggle as a real gust; a model with more resolution would be the way to make them real.
- **Wind direction**: (a) the extra background solves of a turn cost about +2.2 to 2.6 s per 60-minute standard-tier run on this machine; **the phone is unmeasured**, and the fallback is to raise `WIND_TURN_STEP_DEG` from 15 to 20 degrees (fewer stamps, a coarser turn). (b) The 3-D lag with a **fire present**, and on the **High tier**, was **not measured**. (c) **The Danger-gate gap:** for a change scheduled later, a forecast swing that falls after the change starts can still raise a Danger card before the change begins; cards raised before the change applies stay as history; the forecast's S13 and foehn cards still read the forecast direction. (d) No "wind change coming" card, no Danger zone and no "Back to the forecast" for a change of your own (ideas for the owner). (e) The speed cannot be changed any more (the old tool's speed and radius went with it; a belt-kit reading keeps its own). (f) A change or a removal re-runs the simulation from the change's time (up to one checkpoint interval of 1,800 s earlier), the same as an ignition.
- **Existing issues seen again and left alone:** the compass button's tap sometimes misses at 844 x 390 (pre-existing since 0.5.0, not a 0.6.0 change, not fixed); `flowGlyphs`'s xor-hash has a collision weakness; the old Wind streaks ignore reduced motion; the legend card still shows at level 0; F2 of 20.4 (rows near the horizon at L3) stands.
- **A small inconsistency:** the debug statistics' "Wind glyphs" row shows a level-4 row when only the Wind streaks are on (the layer plans the level for them), though the arrows are off.
- **The OrbitControls private-member dependency** (21.3 item 9): `_rotateLeft` and `_handleTouchMoveDollyRotate` of three r186.
- **Owner questions that stand:** whether the APK may drop the **in-thread engine and the demo engine** (they stay: D10); whether the fast tier's *Use the 3-D atmosphere* button should move above the controls (20.5); and, new, whether the detailed streaks should **stop after a minute without a touch** to save battery (answered no for 0.6.0, an idea for the owner).
- **Not built:** E1b (colour the map by speed) and E2 (the 60 m fire-felt raster): 0.5.1 was never authorised; 0.3.1 (rock in the fire model and the speed test button) waits for the S6 re-label.

### 21.5 Decisions taken for you in 0.6.0 (change any of them)

These were taken on the owner's behalf, by the orchestrator in the lane files and by the lanes where nothing was said; **none was confirmed with the owner**, and each is easy to change. **The release number**: 0.6.0, versionCode 10, a minor release for five requests (E1b, E2 and 0.5.1 are not part of it). **The chip's limiting reasons**: the zoom, the model's cells, the drawing budget, the fast tier's estimated wind and reduced motion (21.3 item 7); eye level and both layers off hide it. **"Most detailed level" is L4** of the 0.5.0 ladder (90 px a model cell), not a new level and not a new switch. **Both switches feed one picture at L4**: "the wind streaks" means the animated Wind streaks (near the ground) and the arrows and flow lines alike. **The detailed streaks may go over the frame's triangle limit** (by at most 24,000 triangles at the High tier), because the owner's request is a picture at the closest zoom and a dense forest already fills the frame without it; and **they are about 32 px apart at every zoom**, a first guess to be judged on a phone. **The closest zoom may show eddies the model does not calculate**, and the sheet says so ("added for looks, from the shape of the ground"); the colour stays the model's speed and the direction and speed along a streak stay the model's. **The streaks move faster than the real wind** so that they can be seen, and the sheet says that too. **No idle stop** of the animation (an idea for the owner). **The chip is not shown for the Wind speed map**, in the 2-D map, at eye level (arrows only) or while a panel covers the map, and it sits bottom left above the legend and not near the round buttons. **The two-finger turn is inverted for touch only**; the mouse's right-drag and eye level are as they were. **The slice's density is doubled in both modes** (the moving streaks and the still arrows) **and on the 3-D curtain** (its arrows: 21 columns by nine heights, 168 on a flat line against 75). **Wind direction semantics**: the whole map, not a place; the turn starts at the chosen time (default now, a whole minute, never before the time you look at); 15 minutes (`WIND_TURN_S`), the short way round, eased, stamped at most every 15 degrees; the direction then holds until the next change and replaces the forecast's later swings; the speed is untouched; the whole column turns; no "wind change coming" card, no Danger zone and no "Back to the forecast"; **Remove is "as if never added"** (the run is computed again from the change's time); the belt kit's local readings stay, are listed and removed from the panel and keep their own direction near them; the dial opens on the wind as it is now, and a dial left on the wind says "Hold wind from NW" (keeps the direction, drops the forecast's later changes); the last minute of a scenario takes no change; the tool is called just "Wind direction" everywhere. **A forecast swing that comes before a change starts is still shown.** **The existing bundle budget is reused**: entry 195 kB and all JS 1000 kB gzip are unchanged and no check was weakened. The in-thread engine and the demo engine stay in the APK (D10 default). The version in `package.json` and `android/app/build.gradle` stays 0.5.0 (versionCode 9) until the release is cut, so a debug build of this tree still says 0.5.0. Commit trailers carry no model name.

### 21.x Verification

**The release gate, on the tree at `08cf01d` (source as merged at `fe3a07b`; the commits after it change only markdown), run by the orchestrator on 2026-10-30 on a 4-core software-rendering machine (nothing on a phone):**

| Gate | Result |
|---|---|
| `npx tsc --noEmit` | clean (18 s) |
| `SLOW=1 npx vitest run` | 267 files passed, 6 skipped; 4,775 tests passed, 1 expected fail, 34 skipped (668 s) |
| `npm run build:cap`, `npm run check:bundle` | ok: entry 172.7 kB gzip (budget 195), all JS 982.3 kB (1000), CSS 130.9 kB (135) and 23.9 kB gzip (25), demo data 33.1 MB (36), none of the 4 left-out decoders found. No budget was raised (0.5.0 ended at 169.2 and 963.5) |
| `npm run check:contrast` | ok: every checked pair meets its threshold |
| `node scripts/layout-budget.mjs` | ok at 390x844, 412x915, 360x740, 360x640 and 844x390 |
| tap-target audits (Setup, then the run at 412x915, 844x390 and 360x640, mock engine) | ok: every visible control has a 44 x 44 px hit area |
| `npx playwright test` (170 tests, 2 workers, Pixel 7 profile, software GL) | first full run: 163 passed, 7 failed (80 minutes, with seven other agents using the machine, load average 9 to 15); the 7 were re-run one at a time: 6 passed (`air-tool.spec.ts:151` a browser that did not start in time, `airslice-panel.spec.ts:247`, `app.spec.ts:74`, `layers.spec.ts:344`, `pace.spec.ts:72`, `wind-detail-render.spec.ts:329`: all timing under load, the known load-sensitive set plus two new ones that passed alone). **One fails alone and is not a regression:** `air-tool.spec.ts:439` ("Side view and Map view tabs"), whose last step checks that the old CSS rules would have broken the word "view" across two lines; in this environment the word does not break. It fails the same way on the 0.5.0 tree (`git archive b7baf25`), so it is environmental (fonts and widths of this machine), not caused by 0.6.0 |
| debug APK | see the release commit and the final report |

An earlier gate run on the same tree was lost to a container restart during its Playwright step (steps 1 to 10 had passed); only the Playwright step was repeated. The gallery specs of that run rewrote `docs/screenshots/`; only the pictures the 0.6.0 changes show were kept (05, 09, 13b, 18, 33 to 41 and their dark and high-contrast versions), the rest were restored. **No new gallery picture was made for the chip, the detailed streaks or the Wind direction panel in 0.6.0** (there is no `gallery 0.6.0` test yet); that is a task of 0.7.0's docs.

**Verifier verdicts, from their hand-backs:** Wind direction (engine, session, panel): pass, one should-fix fixed (the plan line on a 360 x 640 phone, `9c228dc`), and the findings F2 to F4 fixed by the W-fix lane (21.1). Two-finger turn: pass, mirrored frame by frame against 0.5.0 on the real app, everything else bit-identical. Side view and slice density: pass with findings (the curtain's arrows, doubled by the orchestrator in `4a9573e`; arrows 29 to 42 % shorter, a floor of about 8 px is a follow-up). Wind detail chip: pass. Detailed streaks: pass with fixes (spacing, the High-tier budget cliff, the density pop: the D-fix lane, 21.3 items 17 to 20). **Phone check-list 4 (section 12): not done; nothing has been seen on a phone.**
